# The base layer

*Every work's own explanation of its part of the world, in the order the book walks them.*

Assembled 2026-09-02T14:04:22 by `tools/build_base_layer.py`. Do not edit this file — it is derived.

Ada Research is a tutorial gone strange: each artifact explains one part of its own
world, beginning at the point and ending somewhere in non-Euclidean space,
irreducibility, QFEP. That explanation was already written — it lives in each work's
own script header — and this is the first time it has been put in order.

| | |
|---|---|
| works in the book | 805 |
| speaking through an `@identity` block | 617 (77%) |
| speaking through leading prose | 62 |
| **silent — no header of their own** | **126** |
| with a wall text | 701 |
| with a reflection | 217 |
| prose assembled | 637 KB |
| documentation cut away | 1089 KB |

A silent work is still listed, with its name and its silence. A work dropped for
having nothing to say is a work nobody will think about again.

---

## 1 · primitives — *F_order*

> Foundation - points, lines, planes, and basic vectors

### point  <span>·</span>  `Point_One`

#### Folding Past

`folding_past`

> You arrive late.  
> The prerequisites are in, stacked away behind you, and the fold keeps running whether or not you accepted the challenge. Everything here arrived late.  
> You are here now.

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### frame_counter_display

`frame_counter_display`

> The internal clock is running, The frame counter updates with the cycle.

**essence** — label.text = Engine.get_process_frames() — a live counter of rendered frames since startup

**desire** — learner understands that VR runs as a discrete sequence of frames, not a continuous flow

**truth** — there is no continuous time in real-time 3D — only discrete frame steps, typically 72 or 90 per second in VR

**emerges** — the frame as the fundamental unit of VR time — everything the player experiences is discretised into frames

**critical_parameter** — reading — whether the panel reports the running total, the rate, the width of one frame, or the overrun against the VR budget

#### Point Zero

`origin`

> Mark the origin.  
> A point made special by convention: not chosen into existence, chosen to be called zero, so everything else has something to be measured from. Zero is already surrounded, so you have somewhere to stand.  
> No turning back.  
>   
> var origin := Vector3.ZERO

**essence** — (0, 0, 0) — the reference from which all coordinates are measured, made visible as a slowly-rotating cyan octahedron with cycling alias labels

**desire** — learner viscerally locates themselves relative to the world's fixed anchor — understanding that "zero" is a name we agreed on

**truth** — the origin is a convention, not a place — but every space must agree on one. The rotating octahedron makes the convention legible: a chosen object at a chosen zero.

**emerges** — understanding that "zero" is a choice, not a given — every coordinate system has an origin and they could have agreed on a different one

**critical_parameter** — spinning octahedron at origin, cycling alias labels (origin / home / zero / (0,0,0) / Vector3.ZERO and 8 more)

*Reflection.* A corner, not a cell. Four cells meet here and none of them contains it, which is why nothing could be centred on the origin until the basin was made even: an odd one has a middle cell, and a corner is not a cell. Three of its four quadrants lie in coordinates no map row can name — the building can stand there, the language cannot. So it was not placed but excavated, two metres down under glass, and became visible by subtraction. While it was being dug, five systems walked into it: a bench, a column, a cornice, a joint, a lit strip. To each of them this was cell (0,0), ordinary floor. It is only the origin because someone says so.

#### You Are Here

`you_are_here`

> You are here

**essence** — a text decal on the floor — the words YOU ARE HERE lying flat under your feet, the way they are printed on a mall map, a transit platform, a parking garage, the floor of a museum lobby. Not a beacon, not a pin: a flat indexical inscription you stand ON. The most-printed sentence in wayfinding, the phrase that only means anything when a body is over it. The point reduced to language and laid into the ground — position spoken as a command and a comfort at once.

**desire** — it wants the player to look down and find themselves already addressed — "here" is wherever they are reading it. It wants the banality of the floor-sticker to do the conceptual work: that "here" is not a place but a relation between a body and a coordinate, printed in advance for whoever arrives. It wants to put the wayfinding decal in drag — to let the most corporate, most neutral floor-text carry the queer charge of "you, specifically, are located."

**truth** — a point is position without extension — and "you are here" is what a civilisation writes when it agrees to render every body as a coordinate it has already labelled. The decal helps you and locates you in the same breath; that is the friendliest face of algorithmic capture, the fence shaped like an arrow pointing at your own feet. Laid on the lab floor, flat and calm, it asks the player to notice they were expected.

**emerges** — alone on a lab floor it reads as orientation; placed over the floor-window above the origin it becomes a caption for coordinate zero — "you are here" written across the glass you can see the point through. Beside `origin` (the absolute 0,0,0) it stages the difference between THE origin and the indexical here: one zero, many bodies, each standing on the same four words.

**critical_parameter** — deixis — what the inscription claims (words_and_mark / words_only / mark_only / arrow / plan_fragment): whether the sentence and the mark that gives it a referent are both on the floor, only one of them is, or the whole thing has been moved inside a drawing of the room. Under it: text + pride_gradient. text is the inscription (default "YOU ARE HERE"); change it and the floor speaks differently. pride_gradient off is the neutral civic decal (one flat colour, the platform's voice); on, the letters run the spectrum — the located self reclaimed from the wayfinding system that named it.

#### 1.0.1 Interactive Point with Origin Line

`interactive_point_origin`

> Point zero, now an object in the exhibition.  
> The sphere is rendering help. The point is the Vector3 that was passed in.  
>   
> point.mesh = SphereMesh.new()

**essence** — p = (x, y, z) — a position you can hold in your hand, relative to origin

**desire** — learner feels coordinates as embodied distance from origin, not abstract numbers

**truth** — a coordinate is a measurement from origin — hold the point, feel the measurement

**emerges** — haptic pulse as a format-change event — the body learns before the mind

**critical_parameter** — the live line drawn to (0,0,0) while held — distance made visible

*Reflection.* Three floats. One position. No extent.

#### Science Screen

`science_screen`

> the map is not the territory — move the point, watch its shadow

**essence** — a SubViewport renders the grid-state of nearby artifacts as a 2D pixel-map on a large wall-mounted screen — you stand in the 3D world and see its cellular logic flattened beside you

**desire** — to make the abstract legible without leaving the embodied — the learner walks the algorithm and simultaneously reads it; the gap between 3D experience and 2D representation IS the research question made visible

**truth** — the map is not the territory — science_screen shows a flattened version of the algorithm you are already inside, and the difference between the two is where understanding lives

**emerges** — point_mode transforms the screen into a live coordinate tracker — it shows the trajectory of a grabbed point in relative space, turning a simple grab into a navigation display

**critical_parameter** — scan_radius (8.0m) — determines which artifacts the screen can detect and mirror; too small and it misses wall-placed artifacts, too large and it picks up unrelated neighbors

#### CoordinateSystem3M

`CoordinateSystem3M`

> Declare the three axes.  
> Red, green, blue. They are unit vectors — they name directions, not places.  
> Nothing is here yet, and you are already in it.  
>   
> const AXIS_X := Vector3(1, 0, 0)

**essence** — X=red, Y=green, Z=blue axes at 3m length — the standard right-handed coordinate frame made visible, now built from the CoordinateLine primitive so every axis carries the same tick-mark ruler vocabulary

**desire** — learner stands inside the coordinate system and feels orientation as embodied direction

**truth** — a coordinate system is a choice of three mutually perpendicular directions with an agreed origin — and the rulers along those directions tell you what the choice actually measures

**emerges** — the arbitrary nature of axis conventions — right-handed vs left-handed, Y-up vs Z-up are choices; AND tick marks make the chosen frame's scale legible

**critical_parameter** — axis_length = 3.0 — at half-scale (scale 0.5) these appear as 1.5m axes in world space

*Reflection.* A point is that which has no part; its place is entirely borrowed from a coordinate system that is not itself a point. For later in color we must give ths more queer colors.

#### Fontana Puncture

`fontana_puncture`

> the one point, pierced

**essence** — a solid cube with a sphere subtracted from it — and the sphere is BIGGER than the cube, so the void opens a round mouth on every face and hollows the matter from inside. Lucio Fontana's Concetto Spaziale carried into three dimensions: not a slash in a flat canvas but a spherical absence carved through solid mass, the "infinite dimension" he said he had created, here literally larger than the object that holds it. At the dead centre, suspended in the hollow it made, floats a single luminous point — the 0-dimensional origin of the cut, the point that ate the cube.

**desire** — it wants to prove the point is generative by SUBTRACTION. Most of the lab adds matter; this one removes it, and the removal is the form. It wants the player to walk around a cube and find it is mostly hole — to see solid red matter with windows on all six faces and understand that the void is not nothing, the void is the work. It wants to make absence sculptural and queer: the cut that is also an opening, the wound that is also a window.

**truth** — a point is position without extension — but give it a radius and aim it at solid matter and it becomes the most consequential thing in the room: the absence that defines the form. Fontana cut the canvas to let the infinite in. Here the infinite is a sphere bigger than the cube, and the cube survives only as the frame that proves there was something for the void to take.

**emerges** — a small sphere reads as a Fontana buchi (a hole); a large one reads as the void winning — the object mostly gone, the dimension behind it now in front of it. Beside `klee_walking_point` (the point that moves) this is the point that DESTROYS-and-reveals; together they teach that a point is never inert — it walks, or it cuts.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_radius vs cube_size. When sphere_radius is small the cube is barely dimpled (sealed, mode-collapse). As sphere_radius grows past the cube's half-extent the void breaches every face and the cube becomes a skeletal frame around an absence — the difference forced all the way through the matter. sphere_offset slides the carving off-centre for an asymmetric scoop.

#### Floating Sphere Field

`floating_sphere_field`

> floating spheres all around me

**essence** — a sparse field of soft glowing spheres drifting in the void around the player — the SUBTLE successor to the Kusama dot-grid. Where the grid asserted a rigid lattice ("the grid IS the aesthetic"), this is the grid exhaled: the same "forms exist in space before they have shape" reading, but as a few dozen luminous motes hanging in air, breathing on a slow drift instead of snapping to coordinates.

**desire** — the field wants to be ATMOSPHERE, not architecture. It should be felt at the edge of vision and never counted. A point-grid says "here is structure"; this says "here is room, and the room is not empty." It wants to make the void legible as a place without filling it.

**truth** — the void was never empty — it was under-rendered. The grid made the void countable; this makes it inhabited. To replace a lattice with a drift is to trade the comfort of knowing exactly where everything is for the truth that presence does not require a coordinate.

**emerges** — low count + large bounds + slow drift = "ambient field, barely there"; raise emission and tighten bounds and it becomes "a swarm of fireflies"; kill drift and it freezes into a soft 3D starfield; push bead_fraction toward 1 and the light in the room turns to gravel.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_count — the entire reading hinges on sparsity. A few dozen (~40) reads as "a presence in the air"; a few hundred reads as fog or snow and the subtlety collapses. Sparse is the point. The Kusama grid was overwhelming-by-repetition; this is its inverse — presence-by-scarcity.

#### grab_sphere_point_snap

`grab_sphere_point_snap`

> A point you can take; it snaps.

**essence** — snap(position) = round(position / grid_size) * grid_size — discretise continuous space

**desire** — learner feels the tension between analog hand movement and digital quantized grid

**truth** — quantization is a choice about what differences are worth keeping

**emerges** — the grid as a constraint that makes space countable; infinite positions become finite choices

**critical_parameter** — grid_size — the quantization step that snaps position to nearest lattice point

#### 1.0.2 Interactive Point — Force Catalyst

`interactive_point_origin_force`

> Grab the ball.  
> “Show me what you can do with those hands” — but the hands are no different from the ball. Both are code. What sits between them is you.  
> One step at a time was the promise. We are already Alice.  
>   
> pickable.picked_up.connect(_on_picked_up)

**essence** — pickable_point + held → vertex-shader morph into force field that drags nearby RigidBodies inward; held + two_hands_close → fires luminous projectile balls

**desire** — the body learns the relation point↔force↔gesture — same single artifact passing through three states as the player engages

**truth** — a point is the seed of a force, a force is the seed of a gesture — same artifact, three nested capabilities, each emerging only when its embodied condition is met.

**emerges** — capability-as-gesture — the artifact's force-field powers exist only WHILE you hold it AND keep both hands engaged. Drop it or part your hands and the field collapses back to a point.

**critical_parameter** — morph_t (0 = point, 1 = force-field shell); attraction_radius + attraction_strength (inverse-square pull); OrbGestureDetector two-handed trigger (balls only fire from a sustained two-hand pose)

*Reflection.* The coordinates change as the point moves. The point's identity does not.

#### Lab Room

`lab_room`

**essence** — a procedurally-generated white modern test chamber that frames a workbench. The room IS the staging — white tile floor, walls, accent-colored strip naming the QFEP phase, signage, observation glass, and a central plinth where the instrument sits. Half-Life test chamber as architectural vocabulary

**desire** — every workbench gets a stage that signals "this is the instrument; the room exists to display it"

**truth** — the room is not the experiment. The room is the SIGNAL that an experiment is about to happen.

**emerges** — a generic room becomes a specific chamber by changing DNA. Same script, different DNA = different room. Change premises and the same instrument is being proved, made, taught, or abandoned.

**critical_parameter** — premises — what kind of knowledge-place the room declares itself to be (chamber | workshop | theatre | ruin); witness — how much of the observation boundary is glazed, i.e. whether a result made here can be checked from outside or must be taken on report (none | port | sash | pane); accent_color — the QFEP phase of the workbench inside, which belongs to the instrument and not to the room, and which neither axis touches.

### lines  <span>·</span>  `Point_Lines`

#### Do Not Cross — Barrier

`do_not_cross_barrier`

> Do not cross this line ...

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Klee Walking Point

`klee_walking_point`

> A line is a point that went for a walk.

**essence** — a single luminous point that sets itself in motion and leaves a trail of points behind it — and the trail IS a line. Straight off the first page of Klee's Pedagogical Sketchbook: "an active line on a walk, moving freely, without a goal. A walk for a walk's sake." The point crosses an invisible sheet of paper hung in the lab, draws its line out of nothing, holds the finished line for a beat, dims, and walks again. The line is never given — it is always the RECORD of a point that moved.

**desire** — the point wants to prove the line is not a primitive. The line is a biography. It wants the player to stop seeing "line" as a thing and start seeing it as a verb — a walk, a duration, a decision repeated. It wants to put Klee in drag: the walk that wanders (meander) against the walk marched to its destination (straight), and to make the wander obviously the richer line.

**truth** — a point is position without extension; a line is a point that refused to stay still. Klee knew drawing is time made visible. In a world built from Vector3 this is the most honest object in the lab: it shows you the geometry you walk through is not a set of shapes but a set of MOVES, and that the straightest move is the one that learned the least.

**emerges** — watched once it teaches point -> line. Watched as a loop it teaches that EVERY line in the whole VR world is a frozen walk. Place a "straight" one beside a "meander" one and the project's core dialectic is staged with no words: the straight line is the line that gave up.

**critical_parameter** — walk_style. "meander" is the queer line — the point wanders by summed sines, no goal, no shortest path. "straight" is the collapsed line — the normalized route that learned nothing on the way. "spiral" and "arc" sit between. Switching walk_style switches between difference and mode-collapse. trail_points sets how finely the walk quantises into its constituent points — the discrete pretending to be continuous.

*Reflection.* A line cannot be drawn without two points it did not make, and the instant it measures them it becomes the instrument that makes space addressable.

#### DÃ¼rer Grid

`dgrid`

> grid lines

**essence** — a standing frame strung with a grid of wires — Dürer's drawing screen, the reticule that flattens depth

**desire** — learner sees perspective as a literal projection through a grid, not an optical trick of the eye

**truth** — perspective is a projection through a grid — Dürer put a screen between eye and world to flatten it

**emerges** — the realization that Renaissance perspective was an algorithm — sample the world cell by cell

**critical_parameter** — rows x cols — the grid density that quantizes the continuous scene into drawable cells

#### Walk This Line — Floor Marking

`walk_this_line_marking`

> Walk this line.

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### 1.1 A Line

`line`

> two points is a line — it measures

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### perspective_lines

`perspective_lines`

> perspective line

**essence** — all lines → (0,0,0) — convergence to a single vanishing point as perspective law

**desire** — learner sees how parallel lines in 3D space appear to converge when projected onto a 2D view

**truth** — perspective is not distortion — it is projection, and projection reveals the vanishing point

**emerges** — the vanishing point as the infinite location of all parallel directions — a limit, not a place

**critical_parameter** — the shared origin — all four line endpoints are forced to (0,0,0)

#### Parallel Line Puzzle

`parallel_line_puzzle`

> parallel line

**essence** — ∥ — two lines with the same direction vector, equal distance apart at every point

**desire** — learner understands parallelism as a relationship between directions, not just visual sameness

**truth** — parallel means same direction — a geometric property, not a visual impression

**emerges** — the idea that parallel lines never meet because they share an infinite point at infinity

**critical_parameter** — the snap tolerance — how close endpoints must be to lock into position

#### Laser Sword

`laser_sword`

> laser,

**essence** — a line you hold like a sword — glowing blade extending from a handle, pure directed length

**desire** — to feel direction as something your arm produces — the blade points where you point

**truth** — a directed line segment is a decision about where to point

**emerges** — direction is not abstract — it is where your wrist aims; length is how far your intention reaches

**critical_parameter** — blade_length — how far the line extends from your grip; adjustable by gripping tighter

#### laser_measure

`laser_measure`

> laser,

**essence** — a handheld measurement instrument — a sword-style cylindrical handle with a copper guard, an emissive LCD screen on its top, a precision laser beam from the guard, and a numeric readout that pairs the beam-tip with the number

**desire** — make scale legible by hand — turn empty space into a measurable quantity, in a body that reads as a tool, not as a placeholder cube

**truth** — Measurement is a probe with a number attached. The beam without the readout is decoration; the readout without the beam is abstract. The pairing is the primitive — now made explicit in three places: a readout that rides the hit-point, a ruler that lives along the beam, and a screen on the handle that reads like a real instrument's LCD.

**emerges** — a wide glowing beam that snaps to surfaces, a hit-dot at the intersection, a glowing green-on-black LCD reading in the handle's saddled housing, and a billboarded numeric label AT the hit-point

**critical_parameter** — max_range and unit — they set what the tool can see and how it reports

#### Laser Exploding Sphere

`laser_exploding_sphere`

> laser.

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Grabbable Line

`grabbable_line`

> VR hands — the line in the hand

**essence** — a line segment you hold as one object — grab anywhere along its length to move it through space

**desire** — learner feels the line as a single entity, not two separate points — unity before decomposition

**truth** — you can hold a line before you know it has endpoints

**emerges** — a line is one thing made of two points — holding it whole precedes understanding it as parts

**critical_parameter** — grain — how finely the segment admits it is made of parts, from one unbroken rod to the two boundary points alone; the envelope never changes, the mass inside it does

#### Line Builder 3D

`line_builder_3d`

> here it forms a vector in 3d space

**essence** — a line rendered as a tube through a chain of grabbable point-spheres, rebuilt every frame from their positions

**desire** — learner feels that a line is not a stroke but a sequence of points — move a point and the line follows

**truth** — a line is a decision to connect points — the points are given, the connection is authored

**emerges** — the insight that a curve is an interpolation over samples — continuity is manufactured between discrete handles

**critical_parameter** — the point positions (point_count and their live global_position) — the line is nothing but the points it threads

#### Light Rod

`lightrod`

> A row of light rods is a punctuation mark for space

**essence** — a row of glowing vertical rods placed by a single config string — the cheapest light-as-line primitive

**desire** — get a measured rhythm of light from a single token (lightrod#config:N:spacing) without touching scene tree

**truth** — A row of light rods is a punctuation mark for space — they don't illuminate the room, they tell you how to read it.

**emerges** — a colonnade of cyan glow strokes that read as both structure and decoration

**critical_parameter** — count and spacing — they define a discrete rhythm of vertical lines in the map

#### scale_lines

`scale_lines`

> every measurement is relative — scale only makes sense from a chosen reference

**essence** — [100m, 10m, 1m, 10cm, 1mm] — a logarithmic ladder of reference lengths made tangible

**desire** — learner viscerally understands the orders of magnitude separating human-scale from micro and macro

**truth** — every measurement is relative — scale only makes sense from a chosen reference

**emerges** — the body as unit — standing next to 1m makes all other lengths relative to your own height

**critical_parameter** — the 1m line — the human-body reference; all others derive meaning relative to it

#### Science Screen

`science_screen`

> the map is not the territory — science_screen shows a flattened version of the algorithm you are already inside

**essence** — a SubViewport renders the grid-state of nearby artifacts as a 2D pixel-map on a large wall-mounted screen — you stand in the 3D world and see its cellular logic flattened beside you

**desire** — to make the abstract legible without leaving the embodied — the learner walks the algorithm and simultaneously reads it; the gap between 3D experience and 2D representation IS the research question made visible

**truth** — the map is not the territory — science_screen shows a flattened version of the algorithm you are already inside, and the difference between the two is where understanding lives

**emerges** — point_mode transforms the screen into a live coordinate tracker — it shows the trajectory of a grabbed point in relative space, turning a simple grab into a navigation display

#### Basic Cube Scene

`cube_scene`

> a unit that has never been divided is not proven indivisible

**essence** — the reference unit. A 1 m magenta-wireframe cube that stands in 155 rooms as the thing every other size, every other transformation, every other body is read against. It is the ruler, not the exhibit.

**desire** — to be so plain that nothing about it competes for attention — and, now, to be able to say how finely it is made of parts without ever stopping being 1 m across

**truth** — a unit that has never been divided is not proven indivisible — only unasked. `grain` is the question 155 rooms forgot to put to their own ruler.

**emerges** — same 1 m envelope, five masses — a solid, a cut, an eight, a twenty-seven, a wireframe made of matter

**critical_parameter** — grain — from one unbroken body to twelve edges. The silhouette is constant; the number of pieces is not.

#### Dark Sphere

`dark_sphere`

> some things in a space exist not to be used but to be sensed

**essence** — a dark semi-transparent sphere pulses with purple emission and wobbles slowly — it marks the inhabited space without asserting itself, a witness to the algorithms around it

**desire** — to be always present but never dominant — the learner barely notices it, but its absence would make the space feel empty

**truth** — some things in a space exist not to be used but to be sensed — dark_sphere is a mood, not a lesson

**emerges** — the halo ring beneath the sphere creates a soft shadow that anchors it to the ground without a hard collision plane

**critical_parameter** — presence (witness) — how much of the room it claims, from hush to eclipse; the old reading named pulse_speed (1.2) and emission range (0.05–0.35), but a rate is invisible in a still and was only ever standing in for this

#### Plus Line Puzzle

`plus_line_puzzle`

> perpendicularity is defined by the dot product equaling zero

**essence** — ⊥ — two lines whose direction vectors have dot product zero

**desire** — learner feels perpendicularity as a physical arrangement they construct with their hands

**truth** — perpendicularity is defined by the dot product equaling zero — geometry expressed as algebra

**emerges** — that 90 degrees is not special until you define an inner product — the dot product makes it special

**critical_parameter** — the intersection constraint — lines must cross at their midpoints to validate

#### modulor_man_demo

`modulor_man_demo`

> a standard human is not a fact about humans. It is a decision about which ones the building will …

**essence** — figure = polyline(MODULOR_UNITS) — a body drawn once, then asked what it is a measure OF

**desire** — stand in front of the Modulor and see the moment a person becomes a ruler

**truth** — a standard human is not a fact about humans. It is a decision about which ones the building will fit.

**emerges** — the same 2.26 m outline reads as a person, a dimension, an ideal, a building grid or an exclusion

**critical_parameter** — measure — body | scale | canon | module | crowd, the apparatus laid over the figure

Half-Life test chamber as architectural vocabulary

#### Floating Sphere Field

`floating_sphere_field`

**essence** — a sparse field of soft glowing spheres drifting in the void around the player — the SUBTLE successor to the Kusama dot-grid. Where the grid asserted a rigid lattice ("the grid IS the aesthetic"), this is the grid exhaled: the same "forms exist in space before they have shape" reading, but as a few dozen luminous motes hanging in air, breathing on a slow drift instead of snapping to coordinates.

**desire** — the field wants to be ATMOSPHERE, not architecture. It should be felt at the edge of vision and never counted. A point-grid says "here is structure"; this says "here is room, and the room is not empty." It wants to make the void legible as a place without filling it.

**truth** — the void was never empty — it was under-rendered. The grid made the void countable; this makes it inhabited. To replace a lattice with a drift is to trade the comfort of knowing exactly where everything is for the truth that presence does not require a coordinate.

**emerges** — low count + large bounds + slow drift = "ambient field, barely there"; raise emission and tighten bounds and it becomes "a swarm of fireflies"; kill drift and it freezes into a soft 3D starfield; push bead_fraction toward 1 and the light in the room turns to gravel.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_count — the entire reading hinges on sparsity. A few dozen (~40) reads as "a presence in the air"; a few hundred reads as fog or snow and the subtlety collapses. Sparse is the point. The Kusama grid was overwhelming-by-repetition; this is its inverse — presence-by-scarcity.

#### Fontana Puncture

`fontana_puncture`

**essence** — a solid cube with a sphere subtracted from it — and the sphere is BIGGER than the cube, so the void opens a round mouth on every face and hollows the matter from inside. Lucio Fontana's Concetto Spaziale carried into three dimensions: not a slash in a flat canvas but a spherical absence carved through solid mass, the "infinite dimension" he said he had created, here literally larger than the object that holds it. At the dead centre, suspended in the hollow it made, floats a single luminous point — the 0-dimensional origin of the cut, the point that ate the cube.

**desire** — it wants to prove the point is generative by SUBTRACTION. Most of the lab adds matter; this one removes it, and the removal is the form. It wants the player to walk around a cube and find it is mostly hole — to see solid red matter with windows on all six faces and understand that the void is not nothing, the void is the work. It wants to make absence sculptural and queer: the cut that is also an opening, the wound that is also a window.

**truth** — a point is position without extension — but give it a radius and aim it at solid matter and it becomes the most consequential thing in the room: the absence that defines the form. Fontana cut the canvas to let the infinite in. Here the infinite is a sphere bigger than the cube, and the cube survives only as the frame that proves there was something for the void to take.

**emerges** — a small sphere reads as a Fontana buchi (a hole); a large one reads as the void winning — the object mostly gone, the dimension behind it now in front of it. Beside `klee_walking_point` (the point that moves) this is the point that DESTROYS-and-reveals; together they teach that a point is never inert — it walks, or it cuts.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_radius vs cube_size. When sphere_radius is small the cube is barely dimpled (sealed, mode-collapse). As sphere_radius grows past the cube's half-extent the void breaches every face and the cube becomes a skeletal frame around an absence — the difference forced all the way through the matter. sphere_offset slides the carving off-centre for an asymmetric scoop.

#### Synthesis Stand

`synthesis_stand`

**essence** — the machine that turns a DNA family back into ONE artifact — a hero (the single variant the measurements argue for, pinned and stood up) or a series (the whole family in measured order of departure from the shipped default, because the progression IS the argument)

**desire** — to close the loop promotion opened. 355 artifacts became families; a family is not placeable, a synthesis is

**truth** — a default is merely the branch that got walked. The hero is the branch the numbers argue for; the series is the family refusing to pick

**emerges** — a hero plinth carries the variant and a plaque stating WHICH values and the measured departure that chose them; a series rail carries default-first-then-ascending, so walking the rail is walking away from the shipped form

**critical_parameter** — subject — which family; mode auto reads the measured verdict from commons/data/dna_synthesis.json rather than asking anyone's taste

### trace  <span>·</span>  `Point_Trace`

#### Mystic Writing Pad

`mystic_writing_pad`

> there is no origin of a trace

**essence** — a child's magic slate standing on the lab floor — Freud's Mystic Writing-Pad (the "Wunderblock", 1925): a red-framed toy with a dark wax slab beneath a translucent grey sheet. A luminous stylus wanders the surface and writes a bright trace out of points; every so often the sheet is "lifted" — the bright surface clears blank again — and the trace it just made does not vanish: it sinks into the wax below as a faint warm ghost, where all the earlier traces already lie, overlapping, a palimpsest of everything that was ever written. The surface forgets. The depth remembers. This is the exact apparatus Derrida used to think the trace ("Freud and the Scene of Writing", 1967): there is no clean page and no pure erasure, only a perception-surface that stays fresh over a memory that keeps the lot.

**desire** — it wants to teach the trace as the play of presence and absence on two layers at once — to show that "clearing the screen" is never deletion, only displacement into a layer you stopped looking at. It wants the player to write, watch the surface wipe clean, and feel the small dishonesty of that clean: the wax got darker. It wants to put the toy in drag as the AI memory and the platform delete-button — the friendly surface that says "forgotten" over a log that forgot nothing.

**truth** — a point is position without extension — and a trace is what a point leaves when time is allowed to pass over it. The Mystic Pad is the most honest object about memory in the lab because it lies the way every memory system lies: it shows you a clean surface and keeps the wax. To learn the trace is to learn that nothing you write is ever only on the surface, and that "clear" is the politest word a machine knows for "kept, where you can no longer see it."

**emerges** — watched once it reads as a toy writing by itself. Watched as a loop it teaches that the line you see is only the most recent of many, and that the blank surface is a claim the wax disproves. Beside `draw_dot` (the hand that writes a single trace) it is the apparatus draw_dot writes ONTO — the memory the gesture lands in; beside `klee_walking_point` (the point that walks a line and dims) it is the point that walks, dims, AND is kept. The Trace map's quiet thesis — "no original behind the trace, only encodings at different scales" — made into a thing you can stand in front of.

**critical_parameter** — clear_interval × ghost_persistence. Short clear_interval with high persistence = the surface is almost always blank while the wax goes black with history — the maximal trace, the unconscious that keeps everything. Long clear_interval with zero persistence = an ordinary whiteboard that truly forgets when wiped — mode-collapse, no depth, no trace, a present with no past. ghost_count is the wax's horizon: how far back the depth still holds before the oldest sinks past recall.

*Reflection.* The trace is everything the line throws away — duration, hesitation, texture — and it is jagged because a resolution is always already there; it has no origin, only a sampling rate.

#### draw_dot

`draw_dot`

> all life is encoded — that is enough

**essence** — sample(hand_position) → ImmediateMesh trail — the hand writes points in 3D space

**desire** — learner experiences themselves as a drawing instrument — hand motion becomes visible line

**truth** — a curve has no intrinsic form — it is just the limit of increasingly dense point samples

**emerges** — the understanding that a continuous curve is just many sampled points

**critical_parameter** — trail_length — how many hand positions are stored before the oldest disappears

#### Lab Room

`lab_room`

**essence** — a procedurally-generated white modern test chamber that frames a workbench. The room IS the staging — white tile floor, walls, accent-colored strip naming the QFEP phase, signage, observation glass, and a central plinth where the instrument sits. Half-Life test chamber as architectural vocabulary

**desire** — every workbench gets a stage that signals "this is the instrument; the room exists to display it"

**truth** — the room is not the experiment. The room is the SIGNAL that an experiment is about to happen.

**emerges** — a generic room becomes a specific chamber by changing DNA. Same script, different DNA = different room. Change premises and the same instrument is being proved, made, taught, or abandoned.

**critical_parameter** — premises — what kind of knowledge-place the room declares itself to be (chamber | workshop | theatre | ruin); witness — how much of the observation boundary is glazed, i.e. whether a result made here can be checked from outside or must be taken on report (none | port | sash | pane); accent_color — the QFEP phase of the workbench inside, which belongs to the instrument and not to the room, and which neither axis touches.

#### Floating Sphere Field

`floating_sphere_field`

**essence** — a sparse field of soft glowing spheres drifting in the void around the player — the SUBTLE successor to the Kusama dot-grid. Where the grid asserted a rigid lattice ("the grid IS the aesthetic"), this is the grid exhaled: the same "forms exist in space before they have shape" reading, but as a few dozen luminous motes hanging in air, breathing on a slow drift instead of snapping to coordinates.

**desire** — the field wants to be ATMOSPHERE, not architecture. It should be felt at the edge of vision and never counted. A point-grid says "here is structure"; this says "here is room, and the room is not empty." It wants to make the void legible as a place without filling it.

**truth** — the void was never empty — it was under-rendered. The grid made the void countable; this makes it inhabited. To replace a lattice with a drift is to trade the comfort of knowing exactly where everything is for the truth that presence does not require a coordinate.

**emerges** — low count + large bounds + slow drift = "ambient field, barely there"; raise emission and tighten bounds and it becomes "a swarm of fireflies"; kill drift and it freezes into a soft 3D starfield; push bead_fraction toward 1 and the light in the room turns to gravel.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_count — the entire reading hinges on sparsity. A few dozen (~40) reads as "a presence in the air"; a few hundred reads as fog or snow and the subtlety collapses. Sparse is the point. The Kusama grid was overwhelming-by-repetition; this is its inverse — presence-by-scarcity.

#### Science Screen

`science_screen`

**essence** — a SubViewport renders the grid-state of nearby artifacts as a 2D pixel-map on a large wall-mounted screen — you stand in the 3D world and see its cellular logic flattened beside you

**desire** — to make the abstract legible without leaving the embodied — the learner walks the algorithm and simultaneously reads it; the gap between 3D experience and 2D representation IS the research question made visible

**truth** — the map is not the territory — science_screen shows a flattened version of the algorithm you are already inside, and the difference between the two is where understanding lives

**emerges** — point_mode transforms the screen into a live coordinate tracker — it shows the trajectory of a grabbed point in relative space, turning a simple grab into a navigation display

**critical_parameter** — scan_radius (8.0m) — determines which artifacts the screen can detect and mirror; too small and it misses wall-placed artifacts, too large and it picks up unrelated neighbors

### line grid  <span>·</span>  `Point_Line_Grid`

#### Room Grammar

`room_grammar`

> space filling

**essence** — binary space partitioning on a floor quad — a rectangle splits recursively into rooms, walls drawn, doors gapped, rooms tinted by area

**desire** — to show that an architectural plan is a grammar — a few recursive split rules generate an entire believable floorplan

**truth** — a floor plan is not designed room by room — it is a rule for dividing space, applied until the space runs out

**emerges** — at higher depths the plan reads as a real building — corridors, small rooms, a logic of enclosure — from nothing but repeated division

**critical_parameter** — grammar — which split production runs, the difference between an accreted building and a barracks; then depth, the lambda between a single hall and a dense warren of rooms

#### player_trace

`player_trace`

> the trace, gridded

**essence** — path(t) — the sequence of positions YOUR body occupies over time, drawn as one growing line; and its derivative, speed, painted into the line's colour so the mark also carries HOW you moved, not only where.

**desire** — the learner sees their own walk become geometry — the body writes in space, and the writing remembers the tempo of the writing. Not a puck on a track: the point that moves is you.

**truth** — every path through space is a function of time, and you are constantly computing it. The trace is the first place the curriculum hands you the pen: your walk is the data, your speed is the ink, and the line is a self-portrait you cannot help but draw.

**emerges** — the realization that motion is a continuous set of points — and that the DERIVATIVE of that set (velocity) is legible, biographical, yours; a line that runs hot where you hurried and cool where you lingered.

**critical_parameter** — trail_max_points — how much of the past is remembered and drawn; the horizon of the biography. (velocity_color scales the reading: speed → hue, motion → mark.)

*Reflection.* The grid arrives promising neutrality and already owning the ground: every cell is an address, and an address is a claim.

`grid_lines`

> grid beauty — slave to the rhythm

Spine-corridor contract — see doc/SPINE_HINTS_CONTRACT.md.
grid_lines reads as "a lattice floating in void" — the cells around it

#### grab_sphere_point_snap

`grab_sphere_point_snap`

**essence** — snap(position) = round(position / grid_size) * grid_size — discretise continuous space

**desire** — learner feels the tension between analog hand movement and digital quantized grid

**truth** — quantization is a choice about what differences are worth keeping

**emerges** — the grid as a constraint that makes space countable; infinite positions become finite choices

**critical_parameter** — grid_size — the quantization step that snaps position to nearest lattice point

#### ConeTwistBag

`ConeTwistBag`

**essence** — cone-twist joint — ball-and-socket with angular limits. Swing span 45°, twist span 15°.

**desire** — To swing within a cone and twist within a narrower one — angular freedom with boundaries.

**truth** — A cone-twist joint draws an invisible boundary in angle-space. The bag knows where it cannot go.

**emerges** — Pendular motion confined to an invisible cone. The bag never escapes its angular prison.

**critical_parameter** — swing_span (45°) and twist_span (15°) — the cone's opening angle and axial rotation limit.

### triangle context  <span>·</span>  `Point_Triangle_Context`

#### Catalyst Pedestal

`catalyst_pedestal`

> the triangle. everything is made of triangles. the quad, the simplest mesh. the pyramid mesh.

**essence** — wireframe_cube(1m) -> display_case(catalyst) -- a glowing cage that presents the crystal

**desire** — to be noticed, approached, and opened — the first thing the player reaches for

**truth** — the cage doesn't protect the crystal — it frames the invitation.

**emerges** — the moment before transformation — the tool exists, waiting to be claimed

**critical_parameter** — cage_color / crystal_hover_height -- the wireframe must say "pick me up"

*Reflection.* The triangle is the smallest figure that cannot be deformed without breaking, so everything is built from it and nothing built from it can bend.

#### Parasol Triangle

`parasol_triangle`

> everything is made of triangles

**essence** — triangle(elevated) + cylinder(stick) — a triangle raised on a pole like a parasol

**desire** — to make triangles visible from across the room — a flag, a marker, an invitation

**truth** — elevate a shape and it becomes a symbol.

**emerges** — the triangle becomes architectural — not just a shape but a structure, a shelter, a sign

**critical_parameter** — sidedness — whether the two faces are painted differently at all, which is the only way a flat sheet can say which way it faces; under it convention (normal / winding), the notation the claim is written in. Then stick_height / triangle_scale — the parasol must read as "triangle on a stick" instantly

#### Triangle Line Puzzle

`triangle_line_puzzle`

> three lines, one triangle

**essence** — triangle = 3 edges connecting 3 vertices — the minimal closed polygon

**desire** — learner discovers that three lines can close into the simplest possible bounded shape

**truth** — three points that are not collinear always define a triangle — and a unique plane

**emerges** — the triangle as the atomic unit of all mesh geometry — every surface is triangles beneath

**critical_parameter** — closure — all 3 snap targets must be occupied with shared endpoints to complete

#### Catalyst Vent

`catalyst_vent`

> the vent is not malicious -- it is the biome leaking

**essence** — vent(rate, wave_size) -> [CatalystFoe ...] -- a corruption seam in the biome that emits foes

**desire** — to be the spawn source of the catalyst tower-defense game in VR -- a place the angry come from, visible from far away

**truth** — the vent is not malicious -- it is the biome leaking; the angry are made of the same biome the player walks on.

**emerges** — a column of grey orbs marching one cell at a time toward the player -- the tower-defense rhythm

**critical_parameter** — emit_interval_s + wave_size -- how fast and how many foes; once wave is spent, the vent goes quiet

#### 1.2: A triangleprofilese

`triangleprofiles`

> triangles, profiled

**essence** — zigzag(n_triangles, height_high, height_low) — a strip of alternating triangles forming a pleat profile

**desire** — learner feels how a complex perimeter shape is just many small triangles sharing edges

**truth** — a complex surface profile is built from the simplest unit — a triangle — repeated with shared edges

**emerges** — the strip as a minimal surface description — pleats and folds arise from alternating vertex heights

**critical_parameter** — grab_point_count — how many vertex handles exist along the zigzag strip perimeter

`catalyst_prompter_box`

`dark_sphere`
