### polythedra  <span>·</span>  `Primitives_Polythedra`

#### grab_trihedron

`grab_trihedron`

> three faces in the hand

**essence** — trihedron — 4 vertices, 3 triangular faces and 1 quad base: the wedge as 3D primitive

**desire** — learner holds a 3D solid, turns it in their hands, and counts faces, edges, vertices

**truth** — a solid is defined by its topology — how faces connect — not by any particular material or color

**emerges** — the Euler relation (V-E+F=2) as something you count by holding; topology felt through handling

**critical_parameter** — button material toggle — pressing reveals the internal structure through a different material

*Reflection.* Five solids and the taxonomy closes; the Platonic set is complete, which is precisely why it cannot hold anything that arrives after it.

#### Path Game Controller

`path_game_controller`

> a game is two truths and a verdict — can you still cross

**essence** — the rulebook of the path-and-block game made into a node — it watches the two ways to win (reach the teleporter, or befriend every builder) and the one way to lose (let the path stay sealed past the clock), and it answers each with a 3D-pixel reaction badge so the outcome is felt, not just scored.

**desire** — to close the loop. The watchdog judges the path; the catalyst flips the foe; this controller is where those two truths become a win or a loss, and where the thumb and the heart get their moment.

**truth** — a game is two truths and a verdict — can you still cross, and did you have to destroy anything to do it. The thumb is the answer to the second.

**emerges** — a thumb blooming over a converted builder is the catalyst's whole thesis in one image — you didn't destroy it, you changed its mind, and the game says yes.

**critical_parameter** — win_on_all_befriended — whether converting every builder counts as a clear. With it on, the game has a pacifist solution (change their minds) standing beside the racer's solution (reach the exit); with it off, only the exit clears.

#### Path Watchdog

`path_watchdog`

> a level is solvable until someone proves it isn't

**essence** — the referee of the path-and-block game — every half-second it asks "does a walkable route still exist from the player to the teleporter?" and when the answer turns to NO it starts a five-second clock that ends the level. It draws the answer as a ribbon on the floor so the player can watch the route narrow and redden as blocks land.

**desire** — to make the maze's topology FELT. A grid of cells is just data until something cares whether you can cross it; the watchdog cares, out loud, on a clock. It wants the loss to be legible — never a surprise, always a thing you saw coming and could have prevented.

**truth** — a level is solvable until someone proves it isn't. The watchdog is that proof, running live — it turns "is there a path?" from a design-time guarantee into a moment-to-moment stake.

**emerges** — with a block-builder foe in the map, the ribbon becomes a live readout of the duel — you and the builder are both editing the same graph, and the red line is the scoreboard.

**critical_parameter** — grace_seconds — the window between "path blocked" and "level restarts". It is the fairness valve: long enough that a momentary seal isn't instant death and you can re-open a route or befriend the builder; short enough that walling you in is a real threat. Five seconds is the felt sweet spot.

#### The Catalyst

`becoming_catalyst`

> interactive cubes — becoming

**essence** — mode(curriculum_progress) -> hand_force(type) -- a held tool that evolves through the curriculum

**desire** — an evolving hand force tracking player progression, from slow cubes to swarm intelligence

**truth** — the catalyst does not change the world -- it changes what the holder can do in the world.

**emerges** — the tool that grows with the learner -- same object, different capability at each understanding stage

**critical_parameter** — current_mode_index / unlocked_modes -- curriculum stage determines the tool's current power

#### pyramid_edit

`pyramid_edit`

> the pyramid mesh

**essence** — pyramid(5 vertices) rebuilt from 5 draggable handles — apex and 4 base corners all movable

**desire** — learner discovers that the pyramid shape is not fixed — every vertex is a degree of freedom

**truth** — a pyramid is just a parameterisation — move the apex and it stops being a pyramid

**emerges** — the intuition that named shapes are just default parameterisations of a more general vertex space

**critical_parameter** — apex handle — moving it changes the character from flat to tall, from pyramid to spike

#### Tentacle Placer

`tentacle_placer`

> the tentacle is the lab's gesture that primitives are FETCHED

**essence** — an off-white animal-robot tentacle anchored on a heavy pedestal. Six FABRIK bones taper like a spine; segmented vertebra rings ring each joint; the tip terminates in a small articulated claw. The Target Marker3D is the one truth — every frame the script smoothly nudges the target toward whatever the cycle's current GOAL is, and a manual FABRIK solver folds the chain so the tip lands on that target. After placing N pyramids the cycle exhausts; the target drifts back to a rest pose and the arm idles, watching.

**desire** — replace "predator that paces" with "experimenter that places, then watches". The placement is finite — three pyramids, then stillness. The room ends up with three new objects, the arm rests, the player can now investigate without interruption.

**truth** — the tentacle is the lab's gesture that primitives are FETCHED — but only a finite number of them. After the show ends, the arm rests. Volume has been authored; the witness can now contemplate it.

**emerges** — the visual rhythm is now CONTINUOUS — target always moving toward its current goal, never stopping at exact times. The arm reads as alive and deliberate rather than stop-motion. The three-pyramid finite scope makes the room a small ritual: one cycle, three objects, then quiet.

**critical_parameter** — place_positions[] — where pyramids land. sky_height — how far up the tip rises between placements. travel_speed — how aggressively the target chases its goal (exponential lerp weight per second; lower = slower, more deliberate). pyramid_size / pyramid_height — shape of the placed primitive.

#### Lab Room

`lab_room`

**essence** — a procedurally-generated white modern test chamber that frames a workbench. The room IS the staging — white tile floor, walls, accent-colored strip naming the QFEP phase, signage, observation glass, and a central plinth where the instrument sits. Half-Life test chamber as architectural vocabulary

**desire** — every workbench gets a stage that signals "this is the instrument; the room exists to display it"

**truth** — the room is not the experiment. The room is the SIGNAL that an experiment is about to happen.

**emerges** — a generic room becomes a specific chamber by changing DNA. Same script, different DNA = different room. Change premises and the same instrument is being proved, made, taught, or abandoned.

**critical_parameter** — premises — what kind of knowledge-place the room declares itself to be (chamber | workshop | theatre | ruin); witness — how much of the observation boundary is glazed, i.e. whether a result made here can be checked from outside or must be taken on report (none | port | sash | pane); accent_color — the QFEP phase of the workbench inside, which belongs to the instrument and not to the room, and which neither axis touches.

#### Basic Cube Scene

`cube_scene`

**essence** — the reference unit. A 1 m magenta-wireframe cube that stands in 155 rooms as the thing every other size, every other transformation, every other body is read against. It is the ruler, not the exhibit.

**desire** — to be so plain that nothing about it competes for attention — and, now, to be able to say how finely it is made of parts without ever stopping being 1 m across

**truth** — a unit that has never been divided is not proven indivisible — only unasked. `grain` is the question 155 rooms forgot to put to their own ruler.

**emerges** — same 1 m envelope, five masses — a solid, a cut, an eight, a twenty-seven, a wireframe made of matter

**critical_parameter** — grain — from one unbroken body to twelve edges. The silhouette is constant; the number of pieces is not.

#### 1.0.2 Interactive Point — Force Catalyst

`interactive_point_origin_force`

**essence** — pickable_point + held → vertex-shader morph into force field that drags nearby RigidBodies inward; held + two_hands_close → fires luminous projectile balls

**desire** — the body learns the relation point↔force↔gesture — same single artifact passing through three states as the player engages

**truth** — a point is the seed of a force, a force is the seed of a gesture — same artifact, three nested capabilities, each emerging only when its embodied condition is met.

**emerges** — capability-as-gesture — the artifact's force-field powers exist only WHILE you hold it AND keep both hands engaged. Drop it or part your hands and the field collapses back to a point.

**critical_parameter** — morph_t (0 = point, 1 = force-field shell); attraction_radius + attraction_strength (inverse-square pull); OrbGestureDetector two-handed trigger (balls only fire from a sustained two-hand pose)

### animatedcube  <span>·</span>  `Point_Animatedcube`

#### animatedcubebuilder

`animatedcubebuilder`

> the cube world, built

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

*Reflection.* The cube is the first form that fills space rather than describing it, so it eats the world it was meant to measure — and until it is folded it is only a flat net.

#### polyhedron_nets_cube

`polyhedron_nets_cube`

> the fold — a cube is a net

**essence** — the 2D nets of tetrahedron, cube, and octahedron that fold up along hinges into their closed 3D solids

**desire** — learner watches a flat strip of faces become a volume — surface area precedes and predicts the solid

**truth** — a polyhedron is a flat thing that folded — the net is not a diagram of the solid, it is the solid unfolded

**emerges** — the understanding that a 3D form is a folded 2D one — every polyhedron is a plane that learned to close

**critical_parameter** — fold_progress (0..1) — the single scalar that carries the shape from unfolded plane to sealed polyhedron

#### Dark Sphere

`dark_sphere`

**essence** — a dark semi-transparent sphere pulses with purple emission and wobbles slowly — it marks the inhabited space without asserting itself, a witness to the algorithms around it

**desire** — to be always present but never dominant — the learner barely notices it, but its absence would make the space feel empty

**truth** — some things in a space exist not to be used but to be sensed — dark_sphere is a mood, not a lesson

**emerges** — the halo ring beneath the sphere creates a soft shadow that anchors it to the ground without a hard collision plane

**critical_parameter** — presence (witness) — how much of the room it claims, from hush to eclipse; the old reading named pulse_speed (1.2) and emission range (0.05–0.35), but a rate is invisible in a still and was only ever standing in for this

#### Science Screen

`science_screen`

**essence** — a SubViewport renders the grid-state of nearby artifacts as a 2D pixel-map on a large wall-mounted screen — you stand in the 3D world and see its cellular logic flattened beside you

**desire** — to make the abstract legible without leaving the embodied — the learner walks the algorithm and simultaneously reads it; the gap between 3D experience and 2D representation IS the research question made visible

**truth** — the map is not the territory — science_screen shows a flattened version of the algorithm you are already inside, and the difference between the two is where understanding lives

**emerges** — point_mode transforms the screen into a live coordinate tracker — it shows the trajectory of a grabbed point in relative space, turning a simple grab into a navigation display

**critical_parameter** — scan_radius (8.0m) — determines which artifacts the screen can detect and mirror; too small and it misses wall-placed artifacts, too large and it picks up unrelated neighbors

#### Floating Sphere Field

`floating_sphere_field`

**essence** — a sparse field of soft glowing spheres drifting in the void around the player — the SUBTLE successor to the Kusama dot-grid. Where the grid asserted a rigid lattice ("the grid IS the aesthetic"), this is the grid exhaled: the same "forms exist in space before they have shape" reading, but as a few dozen luminous motes hanging in air, breathing on a slow drift instead of snapping to coordinates.

**desire** — the field wants to be ATMOSPHERE, not architecture. It should be felt at the edge of vision and never counted. A point-grid says "here is structure"; this says "here is room, and the room is not empty." It wants to make the void legible as a place without filling it.

**truth** — the void was never empty — it was under-rendered. The grid made the void countable; this makes it inhabited. To replace a lattice with a drift is to trade the comfort of knowing exactly where everything is for the truth that presence does not require a coordinate.

**emerges** — low count + large bounds + slow drift = "ambient field, barely there"; raise emission and tighten bounds and it becomes "a swarm of fireflies"; kill drift and it freezes into a soft 3D starfield; push bead_fraction toward 1 and the light in the room turns to gravel.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_count — the entire reading hinges on sparsity. A few dozen (~40) reads as "a presence in the air"; a few hundred reads as fog or snow and the subtlety collapses. Sparse is the point. The Kusama grid was overwhelming-by-repetition; this is its inverse — presence-by-scarcity.

### primitives  <span>·</span>  `Chamber_Primitives`

#### Catalyst Target

`catalyst_target`

> primitives — and so on

**essence** — a destructible practice target for catalyst projectiles — 7 configurable shapes (cube, fireball, apple, banana, barrel, crystal, ring) that explode on hit and respawn after a delay

**desire** — to give the player something to hit — practice targets make the catalyst bracelet legible by providing immediate feedback: you fire, the target breaks, it returns, you try again

**truth** — a target is not an enemy — it is a question; catalyst_target asks whether you can place a projectile in space, and answers with color, destruction, and return

**emerges** — the return — targets respawn with an elastic bounce animation that reads as resilience, not reset; the player learns that the system is cyclical, not final; with a fixture, the support and the mark stay standing while the body is gone, so the empty holder is what you look at during the respawn

**critical_parameter** — shape (via apply_grid_config) — changes mesh, collision, and color palette at runtime; support is what apparatus HOLDS THE BODY UP (none/cradle/frame/gantry built natively, the whole eight-value vocabulary accepted) and mark is the field it is JUDGED IN (none/bullseye/grid/nimbus); hits_to_destroy controls difficulty; moving/orbit enable patrol patterns

*Reflection.* The chamber is where the taxonomy stops being looked at and starts looking back, and the boundary between the one who counts and the thing counted is the first primitive to dissolve.

#### Catalyst Vent

`catalyst_vent`

**essence** — vent(rate, wave_size) -> [CatalystFoe ...] -- a corruption seam in the biome that emits foes

**desire** — to be the spawn source of the catalyst tower-defense game in VR -- a place the angry come from, visible from far away

**truth** — the vent is not malicious -- it is the biome leaking; the angry are made of the same biome the player walks on.

**emerges** — a column of grey orbs marching one cell at a time toward the player -- the tower-defense rhythm

**critical_parameter** — emit_interval_s + wave_size -- how fast and how many foes; once wave is spent, the vent goes quiet

#### The Catalyst

`becoming_catalyst`

**essence** — mode(curriculum_progress) -> hand_force(type) -- a held tool that evolves through the curriculum

**desire** — an evolving hand force tracking player progression, from slow cubes to swarm intelligence

**truth** — the catalyst does not change the world -- it changes what the holder can do in the world.

**emerges** — the tool that grows with the learner -- same object, different capability at each understanding stage

**critical_parameter** — current_mode_index / unlocked_modes -- curriculum stage determines the tool's current power

#### Science Screen

`science_screen`

**essence** — a SubViewport renders the grid-state of nearby artifacts as a 2D pixel-map on a large wall-mounted screen — you stand in the 3D world and see its cellular logic flattened beside you

**desire** — to make the abstract legible without leaving the embodied — the learner walks the algorithm and simultaneously reads it; the gap between 3D experience and 2D representation IS the research question made visible

**truth** — the map is not the territory — science_screen shows a flattened version of the algorithm you are already inside, and the difference between the two is where understanding lives

**emerges** — point_mode transforms the screen into a live coordinate tracker — it shows the trajectory of a grabbed point in relative space, turning a simple grab into a navigation display

**critical_parameter** — scan_radius (8.0m) — determines which artifacts the screen can detect and mirror; too small and it misses wall-placed artifacts, too large and it picks up unrelated neighbors

### ignorance  <span>·</span>  `Primitives_Ignorance`

#### sphere

`sphere`

> the point, grown in every direction — and still not pi

**essence** — the simplest curved surface — a constant distance from a point, the primitive that introduces continuity to the grid's right-angled vocabulary

**desire** — a configurable sphere primitive that any map cell, compound shape, or pedagogical demo can drop in without hand-rolling SphereMesh sub-resources

**truth** — a sphere is what happens when you stop trusting right-angles. The grid lines on its surface tell you the lattice is still there — just bent.

**emerges** — a smooth curved body that contrasts with the cube's hard edges — the primitive that says "not everything is a corner"

**critical_parameter** — radius — sets the visible scale; together with radial_segments × rings, it controls how "round" the sphere reads against the grid

#### Library Rack

`library_rack`

> a library is not the objects — it is the act of organizing them so they can be found

**essence** — library[i] = registry.artifacts[i].scene — a physical index of computational objects

**desire** — stand before a wall of everything the system can make, organized and labeled

**truth** — a library is not the objects — it is the act of organizing them so they can be found

**emerges** — a periodic table for any domain — shaders, primitives, patterns, hazards, math objects

**critical_parameter** — the collection key — one word selects which registry to display

#### diamonds

`diamonds`

> complex regular patterns emerge from simple repeated rules

**essence** — diamonds[i].rotation_y = twist_rule(i) — a tower of solids each rotated by an accumulating angle

**desire** — learner sees how repetition with incremental rotation creates emergent spiral form from simple rule

**truth** — complex regular patterns emerge from simple repeated rules — the rule is more fundamental than the result

**emerges** — the helix as a consequence of uniform angular accumulation — not designed, derived

**critical_parameter** — twist_rule — HOW the angle accumulates over i; linear gives the helix, fixed gives a stack

#### platonic_grabbables

`platonic_grabbables`

> five solids, and the taxonomy ends. the rest is ignorance

**essence** — the DNA head over the table of five platonic solids — a thin root that restates all seventy-five grabbable bodies in one of three states of showing, touching nothing but material_override

**desire** — to let the canonical claim that form can be perfect be MET three ways, so a learner picking up a dodecahedron can ask whether its perfection is in the volume or in the edge count

**truth** — antiquity thought the five regular solids were the elements themselves. There are exactly five and no more are possible — a scarcity built into space, and one you can only see once the volumes are taken away and the edges left.

**emerges** — the five regular solids as three different arguments — a set of coloured volumes, a set of edge graphs, a set of plates in a treatise — and the recognition that "there are exactly five" is a fact about the edge graphs, not about the volumes

**critical_parameter** — facture — which state of showing every solid on the table is in; every position, scale, colour, collider and pickable setting in platonic_grabbables.tscn is the legacy scene and is never written to

#### Plus

`plus`

> symmetry is a property of the transformation group that leaves a shape invariant

**essence** — cross = central box + 4 arm boxes rotated at 0°/90°/180°/270° — 4-fold rotational symmetry

**desire** — learner recognises the plus symbol as a designed artifact with symmetry group C4

**truth** — symmetry is a property of the transformation group that leaves a shape invariant — the cross has C4

**emerges** — that a cross has the same symmetry as a square — 90° rotation leaves it invariant

**critical_parameter** — symmetry — the four arm lengths are one number today, and that single number IS the C4 group; split it and the group collapses (c4 → c2 → a single mirror)

#### grab_octahedron

`grab_octahedron`

> the octahedron has more symmetry than it first appears

**essence** — octahedron — 6 vertices, 12 edges, 8 triangular faces: dual of the cube, Platonic solid

**desire** — learner holds the octahedron and discovers its symmetry through physical rotation

**truth** — the octahedron has more symmetry than it first appears — it looks the same from 48 orientations

**emerges** — the dual relationship to the cube — 6 face-centers of a cube are the 6 vertices of an octahedron

**critical_parameter** — button material toggle — reveals the 8-face structure through a contrasting material

#### sphere_mid

`sphere_mid`

> resolution is a budget decision — there is no correct polygon count

**essence** — SphereMesh(rings=7, radial_segments=7) — mid-resolution sphere where the trade-off is legible

**desire** — learner sees the transitional zone — faces are visible but the sphere reads as round at a distance

**truth** — resolution is a budget decision — there is no correct polygon count, only contextually sufficient ones

**emerges** — the subjective nature of "enough resolution" — what counts as smooth depends on viewing distance

**critical_parameter** — rings=7 and radial_segments=7 — the middle ground where geometry meets perception

#### Star

`star_primitive`

> a star is not drawn — it is a specific list of vertices and the faces that connect them

**essence** — a five-pointed star built from an explicit list of 22 vertices and the triangle faces that bind them

**desire** — learner grasps that a recognizable shape is nothing but data — a table of points and a table of faces

**truth** — a star is not drawn — it is a specific list of vertices and the faces that connect them; form is data

**emerges** — the insight that "shape" is not drawn but declared — geometry is a data structure before it is an image

**critical_parameter** — the vertex array — move one number and the star deforms; the form lives in the coordinates

#### L Shape

`lshape`

> non-convex shapes require explicit geometry at re-entrant corners

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Hole With Cones

`hole_with_cones`

> a hole is not a thing — it is held by what is around it

**essence** — ground_plane - circular_hole + 3_cones_at_120° — absence as geometry: topology through subtraction

**desire** — learner understands that holes are defined by what surrounds them, not what fills them

**truth** — a hole is not the absence of matter — it is a boundary condition imposed on surrounding matter

**emerges** — the concept of Boolean subtraction — the hole is real space defined by its edge, not its missing content

**critical_parameter** — cordon — how the absence is declared to the next person (cones / tape / barrier / none / covered). Under it, hole_segments: how many triangles fan-triangulate the circular boundary of the hole

#### truncatedtetrahedron

`truncatedtetrahedron`

> a solid cut: what the taxonomy does to what it cannot hold

**essence** — tetrahedron with corners cut — each vertex replaced by a triangular face, adding 4 new faces

**desire** — learner sees truncation as a topological operation: vertices become faces, edges become edges

**truth** — truncation is a functor — it transforms the vertex-edge-face structure systematically

**emerges** — the Archimedean insight that truncation transforms one solid into another with more faces

**critical_parameter** — truncation — how deep the cut goes; the Archimedean family is a one-parameter family

#### sphere_high

`sphere_high`

> there is no smooth sphere in real-time 3D — only denser approximations approaching one

**essence** — SphereMesh(rings=16, radial_segments=16) — a sphere approximated by many small triangles

**desire** — learner sees that a smooth sphere in 3D is a polygon mesh — smoothness is a resolution choice

**truth** — there is no smooth sphere in real-time 3D — only denser approximations approaching one

**emerges** — the point where more polygons stop being visually distinguishable — the perceptual limit

**critical_parameter** — rings and radial_segments — together they determine polygon count and visual smoothness

#### righttriangle

`righttriangle`

> the right angle is the basis of all rectangular coordinate systems

**essence** — right_triangle(base, height) with 90° angle at origin — the geometric primitive of Pythagoras

**desire** — learner has a reference form for right angles to which other geometry is compared

**truth** — the right angle is the basis of all rectangular coordinate systems — it defines what "perpendicular" means

**emerges** — the usefulness of black — a neutral diagnostic color that does not compete with adjacent color coding

**critical_parameter** — the right angle at the origin — this defines the shape; moving it makes it non-right

*Reflection.* The sphere is the question the triangle cannot answer — how many faces — and the answer is a budget, so ignorance here is not missing knowledge but a resolution someone chose.

#### sphere_low

`sphere_low`

> the low-poly sphere is more honest than the high-poly one

**essence** — SphereMesh(rings=1, radial_segments=10) — a coarse sphere approximation revealing polygon faces

**desire** — learner clearly sees the triangles that constitute the sphere — the illusion of roundness dissolves

**truth** — the low-poly sphere is more honest than the high-poly one — it does not pretend to be smooth

**emerges** — that "low poly" is not a failure — it is a deliberate aesthetic that exposes the underlying geometry

**critical_parameter** — rings=1 and radial_segments=10 — the minimum that still reads as "sphere-like"

#### capsule

`capsule`

> a capsule is a cylinder that had to be capped so nothing would be sharp

**essence** — a minimal spin driver that turns whatever it is attached to at a fixed degrees-per-second on each axis

**desire** — learner sees that a solid's third dimension is only legible once it turns — a static silhouette hides depth

**truth** — a solid seen from one angle is a silhouette; only turning it returns the depth the eye cannot infer

**emerges** — the understanding that motion is a reading aid — rotation is how a flat view recovers volume

**critical_parameter** — rotation_speed — the per-axis degrees/second vector; zero it and the form freezes into an ambiguous outline

#### Torus Radials Rings

`torus_radials_rings`

> the torus is topologically distinct from the sphere

**essence** — TorusMesh(rings, ring_segments, inner_radius, outer_radius) — a circle revolved around an axis

**desire** — learner understands the torus as a product of two circles — one for the path, one for the tube

**truth** — the torus is topologically distinct from the sphere — its genus is 1, meaning it has exactly one hole

**emerges** — that the torus has a topology distinct from a sphere — it has a hole and cannot be flattened

**critical_parameter** — ring_segments — tube cross-section resolution; at 4 it looks square, at 32 it looks round

#### roughrock

`roughrock`

> nature's irregularity is not random — it is deterministic noise that we lack the seed to …

**essence** — cube_vertices + deterministic_noise(seed) → irregular polyhedron — controlled randomness

**desire** — learner sees a non-uniform organic shape and understands it as seeded perturbation of regular geometry

**truth** — nature's irregularity is not random — it is deterministic noise that we lack the seed to predict

**emerges** — the concept of procedural generation from seed — infinite variation, deterministic replay

**critical_parameter** — noise_seed — the same seed always produces the same rock; change it, get a different rock

#### Snap Octahedron Puzzle

`snap_octahedron_puzzle`

> the octahedron is the cube's dual — swap faces and vertices and you get the other

**essence** — octahedron = 6 vertices, 12 edges, 8 faces — dual of the cube, all faces equilateral triangles

**desire** — learner places 6 floating points and discovers the shape that emerges from this particular arrangement

**truth** — the octahedron is the cube's dual — swap faces and vertices and you get the other

**emerges** — the dual relationship to the cube — realizing these 6 positions are the face-centers of a cube

**critical_parameter** — 6 snap target positions — form a regular octahedron at ±1 on each axis

#### Capsule Radials Rings

`capsule_radials_rings`

> the capsule is the physicist's preferred solid

**essence** — CapsuleMesh(radial_segments, rings, height, radius) — cylinder with hemispherical caps

**desire** — learner understands the capsule as a cylinder that has been made "safe" at its ends

**truth** — the capsule is the physicist's preferred solid — its hemispherical ends are differentiable at all points

**emerges** — the capsule as the collision primitive of choice — smooth ends prevent snagging in physics

**critical_parameter** — radial_segments — controls how round the cylinder cross-section appears

#### prism_block

`prism_block`

> subdividing a triangle is not the same operation as subdividing a square. The

**essence** — the reference triangular unit — a 1 m prism kept next to a cube so a room has something asymmetric to measure against

**desire** — to show that "one unit" is a claim about wholeness, not about size

**truth** — subdividing a triangle is not the same operation as subdividing a square. The square splits into squares forever; the triangle splits into triangles that point the OTHER WAY. Half the pieces of a subdivided triangle are upside down, and there is no way to make them agree.

**emerges** — the same triangle read five ways — one block, a parted block, four, nine, and nine edges with no faces at all

**critical_parameter** — grain — how many pieces the unit is made of, at constant silhouette

### portals  <span>·</span>  `Primitives_Portals`

#### Achilles and the Tortoise

`achilles_tortoise`

> Achilles and the tortoise: the endlessness of pi in a footrace

**essence** — A_n = L*(1-1/2^n), T_n = L*(1-1/2^(n+1)) — geometric series converging to a finite limit

**desire** — learner feels the paradox in time — watching each step get smaller, knowing they converge

**truth** — infinite subdivision converges — Zeno was right about the steps, wrong about the sum

**emerges** — the resolution — infinite steps sum to finite distance; the paradox dissolves in the limit

**critical_parameter** — the step reveal timing — each step marker appears sequentially, spacing halving each time

*Reflection.* The portal is the edge made walkable: you step through it and arrive at another approach, because pi does not finish and neither does the corridor.

#### combine_portals

`combine_portals`

> a portal: the edge, walked through

**essence** — portals[i].ring_segments = base_segments + i*increment — a progression of torus discretization levels

**desire** — learner walks through a corridor of increasingly refined tori and feels resolution as spatial experience

**truth** — every smooth curve in 3D graphics is an illusion — beneath it are polygons, and you choose how many

**emerges** — the relationship between segment count and perceived smoothness — resolution is a resource allocation

**critical_parameter** — portal_count — how many steps the resolution ladder has; more steps = finer progression

**essence** — a sparse field of soft glowing spheres drifting in the void around the player — the SUBTLE successor to the Kusama dot-grid. Where the grid asserted a rigid lattice ("the grid IS the aesthetic"), this is the grid exhaled: the same "forms exist in space before they have shape" reading, but as a few dozen luminous motes hanging in air, breathing on a slow drift instead of snapping to coordinates.

**desire** — the field wants to be ATMOSPHERE, not architecture. It should be felt at the edge of vision and never counted. A point-grid says "here is structure"; this says "here is room, and the room is not empty." It wants to make the void legible as a place without filling it.

**truth** — the void was never empty — it was under-rendered. The grid made the void countable; this makes it inhabited. To replace a lattice with a drift is to trade the comfort of knowing exactly where everything is for the truth that presence does not require a coordinate.

**emerges** — low count + large bounds + slow drift = "ambient field, barely there"; raise emission and tighten bounds and it becomes "a swarm of fireflies"; kill drift and it freezes into a soft 3D starfield; push bead_fraction toward 1 and the light in the room turns to gravel.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_count — the entire reading hinges on sparsity. A few dozen (~40) reads as "a presence in the air"; a few hundred reads as fog or snow and the subtlety collapses. Sparse is the point. The Kusama grid was overwhelming-by-repetition; this is its inverse — presence-by-scarcity.

### melencolia  <span>·</span>  `Primitives_Melencolia`

#### DÃ¼rer Melencolia Scene

`durer_scene`

> Dürer's polyhedron — the solid no taxonomy names, and the angel who keeps it

**essence** — Dürer's truncated rhombohedron from Melencolia I (1514) — a cube stretched along its body diagonal and cut at two corners

**desire** — learner meets geometry as a mood — the same vertex tables that make a cube can make an object that reads as melancholy

**truth** — geometry is never only geometry — Dürer's solid proves a polyhedron can hold five centuries of meaning

**emerges** — the recognition that a primitive carries cultural weight — a shape can be a symbol, not only a form

**critical_parameter** — size and the golden-ratio proportions (h, w) — the elongation that turns an ordinary cube into Dürer's brooding solid

*Reflection.* Every instrument is present and the set is finished, and still nothing is answered — melancholia is the correct response to a taxonomy that is complete and insufficient at once.

#### Snap Pyramid Puzzle

`snap_pyramid_puzzle`

> the pyramid, snapped — held by the grid

**essence** — square pyramid = 5 vertices, 8 edges, 5 faces (1 square + 4 triangles) — non-Platonic mixed solid

**desire** — learner places 5 points and observes how a square base plus apex forces triangular sides

**truth** — a pyramid has a free parameter — apex height — that tetrahedra and octahedra do not

**emerges** — that the apex position determines whether the pyramid is regular or skewed — height is a free variable

**critical_parameter** — 5 snap target positions — 4 corners of a square base plus 1 apex above center

**emerges** — a readable in-VR text panel that the map-author addresses by tt:<name> tokens

**critical_parameter** — current_tutorial_id — picks which entry from tutorial_text.json is shown

**truth** — a pyramid has 5 vertices but only 1 is special — the apex determines the form

**truth** — a frame is a boundary you can pass through — the hollow is the point, the solid only holds it open

**emerges** — the understanding that a frame is defined by what it removes — the hole is designed, not left over

#### prism_block

`prism_block`

**essence** — the reference triangular unit — a 1 m prism kept next to a cube so a room has something asymmetric to measure against

**desire** — to show that "one unit" is a claim about wholeness, not about size

**emerges** — the same triangle read five ways — one block, a parted block, four, nine, and nine edges with no faces at all

**critical_parameter** — grain — how many pieces the unit is made of, at constant silhouette

**truth** — some things in a space exist not to be used but to be sensed — dark_sphere is a mood, not a lesson

**desire** — the field wants to be ATMOSPHERE, not architecture. It should be felt at the edge of vision and never counted. A point-grid says "here is structure"; this says "here is room, and the room is not empty." It wants to make the void legible as a place without filling it.

**truth** — the void was never empty — it was under-rendered. The grid made the void countable; this makes it inhabited. To replace a lattice with a drift is to trade the comfort of knowing exactly where everything is for the truth that presence does not require a coordinate.

**critical_parameter** — sphere_count — the entire reading hinges on sparsity. A few dozen (~40) reads as "a presence in the air"; a few hundred reads as fog or snow and the subtlety collapses. Sparse is the point. The Kusama grid was overwhelming-by-repetition; this is its inverse — presence-by-scarcity.

#### Basic Cube Scene

`cube_scene`

**essence** — the reference unit. A 1 m magenta-wireframe cube that stands in 155 rooms as the thing every other size, every other transformation, every other body is read against. It is the ruler, not the exhibit.

**desire** — to be so plain that nothing about it competes for attention — and, now, to be able to say how finely it is made of parts without ever stopping being 1 m across

**truth** — a unit that has never been divided is not proven indivisible — only unasked. `grain` is the question 155 rooms forgot to put to their own ruler.

**emerges** — same 1 m envelope, five masses — a solid, a cut, an eight, a twenty-seven, a wireframe made of matter

**critical_parameter** — grain — from one unbroken body to twelve edges. The silhouette is constant; the number of pieces is not.

## 2 · transformation — *F_order*

> Invariants - dot/cross products enable rotation

### trans introduction  <span>·</span>  `Trans_Introduction`

#### Homogeneous Coordinates

`homogeneous_coordinates`

> a point carries a 1, a direction carries a 0 — that is the whole difference

Visualizes homogeneous coordinates — why 4×4 matrices encode 3D transforms.
Displays a color-coded 4×4 matrix: rotation/scale (blue 3×3 block),
translation (green column), projection (red row), and the homogeneous
scalar w=1 (gray). A small 3D coordinate frame demonstrates the transform,
with a VR slider to adjust frame scale interactively.

--- Constants ---

#### Invariants Demo

`invariants_demo`

> what does not change when everything moves

**essence** — an invariant is identity-under-motion — the property a transformation cannot touch. What SURVIVES the move tells you what the move truly is; the thing that holds while everything shifts is where the object keeps its name. This is the QFEP core of transformation: identity is not the frozen shape but the pattern that persists across the whole orbit of a transform.

**desire** — press a button, watch measurements change or hold, and read which transforms respect which properties; then PROJECT one edge onto another and watch the shadow shrink as the angle opens — see the dot product become the length that survives.

**truth** — The properties a transformation cannot touch define the transformation more than the properties it changes; and the dot product is that survival made a number — how much of one thing points along another.

**emerges** — translation and rotation preserve everything; uniform scale preserves angles; shear preserves nothing; and projection makes ALIGNMENT legible — the dot product is the projected length, cos θ is how much of one vector points along another, 1.0 when parallel, 0 when perpendicular.

**critical_parameter** — shear — the only transform here that changes every measurement (the transform with no invariant, identity fully dissolved); and the angle between two edges — the parameter the PROJECT read makes visible as cos θ.

#### Rotation Gimbal

`rotation_gimbal`

> three rings, and one way to lose a degree of freedom

Gimbal lock demonstrator — 3 nested rotation rings (X=red, Y=green, Z=blue)
with VR sliders for each Euler angle. When Y approaches +/-90 deg, X and Z
rings align and "GIMBAL LOCK!" flashes. Inner box shows the final orientation.
Teaches why quaternions matter.

#### Matrix 4x4 Viewer

`matrix_4x4_viewer`

> sixteen numbers. every move you will ever make

**essence** — the matrix is the truth of the transform — numbers become geometry

**desire** — slide translate/rotate/scale, watch the cube warp and the 16 numbers shift

**truth** — Every spatial transformation hides inside 16 numbers — the matrix makes the invisible scaffolding visible.

**emerges** — identity matrix = no change; rotation fills off-diagonal cells; translation only touches the last column

**critical_parameter** — rotation — the only transform that mixes matrix columns

#### Transform Composition

`transform_composition`

> one after another is one. order matters

Transform composition demonstrator — chain Rotate then Translate vs Translate
then Rotate on a simple house shape. The results land in DIFFERENT places,
showing that matrix multiplication is non-commutative: R·T ≠ T·R.
VR sliders control rotation angle and translation distance.
Buttons toggle which result is visible. Animated step-through.

--- DNA (stage 2, promoted 2026-08-03) ------------------------------------
STAGE-2 DNA. One axis: `pair`, WHICH TWO OPERATIONS ARE BEING COMPOSED.
The headline was hard-coded to one case — rotate against translate — and one
case cannot tell you whether the ≠ is a fact about composition or a fact about
these two operators. `rotate_scale` is the value that pays: a rotation about
the origin and a UNIFORM scale about the origin genuinely commute, the two
houses land on top of each other, and the headline flips to "=" on its own
because the code compares the two composed matrices rather than being told.

--- Configuration ---


AXIS — THE TWO OPERATIONS THE DEMONSTRATOR COMPOSES. Order A is always
"first then second"; order B is always the reverse. The four pairs are the
same four the transform_composition_workbench walks on its slider, so the two
siblings argue the same case on the same evidence.
  rotate_translate  rotate about Y, then translate along X. The shipped case:
                    the classic split, two houses in two different places.
  rotate_scale      rotate about Y, then scale uniformly about the origin.
                    THESE COMMUTE. The two finals coincide exactly — but the
                    two ghosts do not, so a still still shows two different
                    roads to one place.
  translate_scale   translate along X, then scale about the origin. Splits:
                    scaling after moving multiplies the offset.
  rotate_rotate     rotate about Y, then rotate about X. Splits — the
                    non-abelian core of SO(3), with no translation in sight.

*Reflection.* One matrix holds every move, but only because a fourth coordinate was added to carry what three could not: the point's 1 against the direction's 0, a whole ontology smuggled in as bookkeeping.

#### Balance Puzzle

`balance_puzzle`

> the transform you cannot see: weight

**essence** — stack(8 pieces) until stable for stability_time seconds → creature emerges

**desire** — learner experiences the sublime of physics — patience and precision rewarded with life

**truth** — stability is not a property of any single piece — it emerges from the relationships between all

**emerges** — the creature as reward for embodied patience; the stack as a meditation on balance under gravity

**critical_parameter** — stability_time — how long the stack must hold before the reward animation fires

### trans translation  <span>·</span>  `Trans_Translation`

#### Transform Composition Workbench

`transform_composition_workbench`

> compose: the hand builds the matrix

**essence** — an interactive transform-composition workbench — the player turns a slider that picks one of four (T1, T2) pairs of transformations, and watches BOTH orderings of that pair applied to a base tetrahedron, side-by-side. Left shape = T2∘T1 (T1 first); right shape = T1∘T2 (T2 first). A ghost of the base sits between them. Two gold trajectory arrows run from the ghost to each result. When the two results coincide (pair 2 — rotation around origin × uniform scale) a "commutes" badge lights up; for the other three pairs the readout says "non-commutative".

**desire** — learner viscerally locates the non-abelian-ness of the transformation group — order is not metadata, it's the operation. The slider walks four pairs and the eye sees that ALMOST all of them split.

**truth** — order is operation. R·T ≠ T·R for most pairs. The slider lets you walk the four cases, and the badge tells you when (and only when) the two operations happen to commute — exactly when both fix the origin and one is a uniform scale.

**emerges** — SO(3) non-abelianness, the "commuting subgroup" of (rotation around origin × uniform scale around origin), and order-dependence under composition — all made into a single hand-driven instrument

**critical_parameter** — pair_index ∈ {0,1,2,3} — the only knob. Picks T1 and T2; both orderings compute live.

*Reflection.* To move is only to add, so the thing arrives as it left — but the void is not filled, only spanned, and the crossing is infrastructure that asks you to stand still and trust it.

#### Z-Translation Cube

`z_translation_cube`

> plus z: deeper

**essence** — position[axis] = start + direction * lerp(0, 1, progress) — pure single-axis translation animated

**desire** — learner reads the label and watches the cube traverse one axis, internalising what "X translation" means

**truth** — translation is the additive group action on position — it changes location while preserving orientation

**emerges** — the orthogonality of axes — moving on X changes nothing about Y or Z, visually confirmed

**critical_parameter** — axis — X, Y, or Z; determines which coordinate changes while the others stay fixed

`pick_up_cube`

> the first transform is the hand's

`y_translation_cube`

**essence** — position[axis] = start + direction * lerp(0, 1, progress) — pure single-axis translation animated

**desire** — learner reads the label and watches the cube traverse one axis, internalising what "X translation" means

**truth** — translation is the additive group action on position — it changes location while preserving orientation

**emerges** — the orthogonality of axes — moving on X changes nothing about Y or Z, visually confirmed

`pickup_gate`

> a move that opens

### trans axisdecomposition  <span>·</span>  `Trans_AxisDecomposition`

#### Torus Cylinder

`toruscylinder`

> a ring and a tube: the same surface, one axis apart

**essence** — torus.rotation_y += dt*speed; cylinder.position.y = sin(t*freq)*amplitude — rotation vs oscillation

**desire** — learner sees two fundamental animated transformations side by side: continuous rotation and periodic translation

**truth** — rotation and translation are both transformations — one preserves distance from a fixed point, the other does not

**emerges** — the sine wave as the path of a constrained oscillator — the trail makes the mathematical curve physical

**critical_parameter** — cylinder_osc_speed — the frequency of the sine wave; doubles the frequency, doubles the loops

*Reflection.* One move is three moves and the order does not matter — but only here, and the distance is not there at all until a body has walked it into being.

#### Translation Cube Demo

`translation_cube_demo`

> the move, taken apart

**essence** — constrained_translation — move only on allowed axis; sequential constraints: first UP, then SIDEWAYS

**desire** — learner physically discovers that translation is axis-specific — the door will not open any other way

**truth** — a translation requires both a direction and a magnitude — constraint is what makes it meaningful

**emerges** — the idea that translation has direction — not all displacement is allowed, only along defined axes

**critical_parameter** — the axis sequence constraint — UP must complete before SIDEWAYS unlocks

#### X-Translation Cube

`x_translation_cube`

> plus x: across

**essence** — position[axis] = start + direction * lerp(0, 1, progress) — pure single-axis translation animated

**desire** — learner reads the label and watches the cube traverse one axis, internalising what "X translation" means

**truth** — translation is the additive group action on position — it changes location while preserving orientation

**emerges** — the orthogonality of axes — moving on X changes nothing about Y or Z, visually confirmed

**critical_parameter** — axis — X, Y, or Z; determines which coordinate changes while the others stay fixed

### trans rotation  <span>·</span>  `Trans_Rotation`

#### Rotate Grid Cubes

`rotate_grid_cubes`

> the grid turns and stays a grid

**essence** — choreographed permutation — rows of grid cubes rotated in axis-banded segments

**desire** — read the grid as a score: 6 rows Z, 4 flat, 6 Y, 4 flat, 6 X-negative, 4 flat, 6 all, 8 flat

**truth** — The grid is a stack of choices. Which axis turns where, in which order, IS the music — randomness lives in the score, not the dance.

**emerges** — a moving stripe of axis-aligned tilts that sweeps through the grid revealing structural anisotropy

**critical_parameter** — PATTERN — the ordered list of (row_count, axis) bands that defines the cycle length

*Reflection.* A turn needs something to turn around, and the moment it happens space stops being the same in all directions: rotation manufactures facing, and now order matters.

#### spin

`spin`

> turning with nothing to turn around

**essence** — MultiMesh phases: translate → translate+rotate → translate — rotation mid-sequence changes everything

**desire** — learner reads the full MultiMesh array as a story: straight, then spinning, then straight again

**truth** — translation then rotation is not the same as rotation then translation — order is not commutative

**emerges** — the non-commutativity of transformation composition — the order of translate and rotate matters

**critical_parameter** — z_rotation_degrees — the per-step rotation angle during phase 2; controls how tight the spiral

### trans rotationspectacle  <span>·</span>  `Trans_RotationSpectacle`

#### Boolean Tunnel

`boolean_tunnel`

> a tunnel is a solid subtracted, turning

**essence** — hollow_cube[i].rotation_z += i * rotation_per_segment — accumulating rotation carves a twisted corridor

**desire** — learner walks through a tunnel whose walls rotate and understands rotation as spatial grammar

**truth** — accumulated rotation is non-linear — each segment adds to all previous, so the total diverges quickly

**emerges** — the architectural experience of rotation as navigation — the body learns angles by moving through them

**critical_parameter** — rotation_per_segment — how many degrees each segment adds; at 0° it is a straight box, at 10° a spiral

*Reflection.* Everything turns and the centre does not, and the long approach makes you pay for the arrival — the eye is the part that actually moved.

#### carousel_cake

`carousel_cake`

> the carousel: everything moves, the centre does not

**essence** — layer_speed[i] = base_speed * multiplier^i — exponential speed escalation across 8 rotating layers

**desire** — learner watches layers spin at different speeds and feels how a single rule creates complex motion

**truth** — geometric progression means small multiplier differences create enormous speed ratios after many layers

**emerges** — visual interference patterns as fast and slow layers pass each other — complexity from simple scaling

**critical_parameter** — rotation_speed_multiplier — each layer is this factor faster than the one below it

### trans scale  <span>·</span>  `Trans_Scale`

#### Chair Assembly Puzzle

`chair_assembly_puzzle`

> parts to chair: every piece a transform

**essence** — place_and_scale(cube_pieces) to match holographic ghost guides — transformations as furniture assembly

**desire** — learner discovers that scale, rotate, and translate together are sufficient to build any rectangular form

**truth** — all furniture is transformation applied to a primitive — the cube is the universal raw material

**emerges** — Judd's insight made playable: "a chair is just rectangles with the right transforms"

**critical_parameter** — furniture_type enum — selects which target configuration (CHAIR/STOOL/TABLE/SHELF/CUSTOM)

*Reflection.* Scaling multiplies and adds nothing, so bigger is not more — and an object that asks to be scaled has been given a desire in order to make your hand feel invited.

#### Clipboard

`clipboard`

> copy, paste — the transform that makes two

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### scale_me

`scale_me`

> times two. the same, and not

**essence** — world.scale *= scale_amount on pickup — the player's reference frame grows, making everything feel tiny

**desire** — learner experiences scale change as embodied perspective shift — not a number, a felt transformation

**truth** — scale is relational — 100× larger means nothing without a reference body to measure against

**emerges** — the phenomenological difference between knowing a scale ratio and experiencing it as body-relative space

**critical_parameter** — scale_amount — at 100× the world becomes monumental; lower values are subtle

### transformation  <span>·</span>  `Chamber_Transformation`

#### Proximity Spawner

`proximity_spawner`

> come close — something appears

**essence** — monitors distance from player each physics frame; spawns enemy_type when player enters spawn_radius, despawns when past despawn_radius, and waits respawn_delay seconds before trying again

**desire** — to make the world feel inhabited — the learner never knows exactly when a creature will appear, so proximity itself becomes a kind of alertness

**truth** — presence is not a property of the creature — it is a function of distance; the spawner makes explicit what is always true: things only exist for you within a radius

**emerges** — respawn_delay creates rhythm — after a creature dies, there is a 2-second silence before the next appears; that gap is where the learner exhales

**critical_parameter** — spawn_radius vs despawn_radius (15m vs 30m) — the asymmetry creates a hysteresis zone where the creature stays alive even as the player backs away; avoids the flickering of a symmetric threshold

*Reflection.* The catalyst does not damage the creature, it changes its state, and the whole argument of the chamber is that folded is not less: a transformation done to a body is contact, not harm.

### trans pit  <span>·</span>  `Trans_Pit`

#### Pusher Block

`pusher_block`

> pushed — translated by the room

**essence** — translation(block) -> push(player) -> pit(death) — a cube that slides and shoves you into the void

**desire** — to make translation felt in the body — not a number but a force that moves you

**truth** — translation is the simplest transformation and the most dangerous — everything that moves can push.

**emerges** — the player learns translation by being translated — the block does to you what you do to objects

**critical_parameter** — push_speed / push_distance — too slow is boring, too fast is unfair, the sweet spot teaches timing

*Reflection.* Here the transform is done to you, and the arithmetic that was neutral while it acted on objects is not neutral once the space it acts on is inhabited.

#### Grower Block

`grower_block`

> grown — scaled by the room

**essence** — scale(block) -> grow(into_player) -> pit(death) — a cube that expands and squeezes you out

**desire** — to make scale felt as pressure — the world growing into your body, stealing your space

**truth** — scale is the transformation that changes everything while appearing to change nothing.

**emerges** — the uncanny transformation — you don't move, the world moves into you

**critical_parameter** — grow_speed / max_scale — the breathing rhythm must be readable: small=safe, big=danger

## 3 · color — *F_order*

> Foundation - colour systems become composition systems, on an index the arrays supply: address, then attribute, then rule, then the made thing

### pattern foundry  <span>·</span>  `Pattern_Foundry`

#### Pattern Machine D — Tile-Stamp Press

`pattern_machine_d`

> a print is a rule pressed into a surface. The matrix is the type, the

**essence** — a TILE-STAMP PRESS — a sci-fi printing press for wallpaper. The player arranges a small NxN stamp matrix on the angled press bed, pulls the press lever, and the head slams down and STAMPS the symmetry-tiled carpet onto a conveyor tray that feeds it off the front of the machine. The 17 wallpaper groups stop being a flat sample and become a print run.

**desire** — to show that a pattern is MADE by a machine and editable in the same breath. pattern_maker_station edits; pattern_loom manufactures; this does both — an editable matrix + a press stroke + carpet rolling off a tray.

**truth** — a print is a rule pressed into a surface. The matrix is the type, the symmetry group is the forme, and the carpet is the impression — pull the lever and the almost-nothing becomes architecture.

**emerges** — the federated move — reuse WallpaperGroups.get_symmetric_color for the live carpet and PatternSim.render_to_image for the proof sheet, then wrap both in an apparatus. The press is a body for a rule.

**critical_parameter** — wallpaper_group — the symmetry operator engraved into the press. The same 9-cell matrix, run through p1..p6m, prints 17 different universes from one arrangement of stamps.

*Reflection.* the press stamps its own carpet but not its own law: the symmetry group is engraved into the forme before a single cell is painted, so the machine prints seventeen universes and cannot ask for an eighteenth.

#### Pattern Printer (Holographic Press)

`pattern_machine_b`

> a pattern is a program plus a fragment. Hand someone the fragment and the

**essence** — a HOLOGRAPHIC PATTERN PRINTER — a sci-fi press that does not show you a finished tiling, it PRINTS one. The player edits a small floating holographic motif grid on the tilted print-head console; the machine symmetrises that fragment through one of the 17 wallpaper groups and EXTRUDES the result two ways at once: a glowing sheet that scrolls upward out of the print head, and a carpet pressed onto the floor.

**desire** — to fuse the editor and the machine. pattern_maker_station lets you paint a motif; pattern_loom manufactures a fixed one. This wants both — you touch the hologram, and the press immediately re-prints the world.

**truth** — a pattern is a program plus a fragment. Hand someone the fragment and the press, and they discover that constraint, not complexity, is where structure comes from. You print architecture out of almost nothing.

**emerges** — the same federated move as pattern_loom — reuse WallpaperGroups + PatternSim PALETTES, wrap them in an apparatus — but here the apparatus is INTERACTIVE. The motif you paint is the cartridge; the printer runs it.

**critical_parameter** — wallpaper_group — the symmetry operator. A 5x5 painted fragment is almost nothing; the group is the program that multiplies it into endless printed fabric. 17 groups, a hard constraint on 2D repetition.

#### Pattern Machine A — Jacquard Punch-Card Loom

`pattern_machine_a`

> the Jacquard loom was the first programmable machine. The pattern was

**essence** — punch_card(NxN pegs) -> wallpaper_group -> woven carpet. The Jacquard insight made literal — the holes in the card ARE the program, and the loom weaves whatever the card says, metre after metre.

**desire** — to let hands punch the card on the machine and watch the loom change what it weaves in real time; to feel that a textile is the OUTPUT of a readable, editable program, not a fixed sample on a plate.

**truth** — the Jacquard loom was the first programmable machine. The pattern was never woven by hand — it was punched into a card and read by the rule.

**emerges** — the federated move — reuse the grammar's symmetry operator + the loom's scrolling-cloth machine, add a hands-on punch card on the head.

**critical_parameter** — the punch card (the motif grid). A handful of toggled pegs, read through one of 17 wallpaper groups, becomes endless fabric.

#### Kaleidoscope Mill

`pattern_machine_c`

> a wallpaper group is not a label — it is a finite set of mirrors. Feed the mirrors

**essence** — kaleidoscope_mill(hopper[x][y]) -> radial_carpet — drop a handful of motif cells into a central hopper, watch spinning mirror wedges multiply the fragment and the mill spit out an infinite wallpaper-group carpet. The symmetry IS the machine.

**desire** — to make wallpaper symmetry physically legible — not a number on a plate but a set of rotating mirrors you feed and watch multiply your fragment into architecture.

**truth** — a wallpaper group is not a label — it is a finite set of mirrors. Feed the mirrors almost nothing and they return a universe. The mill makes the multiplication visible.

**emerges** — the same federated move as pattern_loom — reuse WallpaperGroups + PatternSim, wrap them in an apparatus. But here the apparatus is interactive: the player is the feedstock source, and the mirrors are the algorithm made mechanism.

**critical_parameter** — wallpaper_group — the 17 ways the rotor reflects the hopper into a plane. Cycling the group visibly re-geometries the mill: 2-fold, 4-fold, 6-fold rotors.

#### CSG Architecture Cavity

`csg_architecture_cavity`

> Every architectural cavity is a difference. A room is a wall that has been subtracted …

**essence** — A thick wall with three openings — doorway, circular porthole, arched window — each a CSG subtraction from the same solid. Architecture as Boolean difference.

**desire** — To turn the difference operator into a habitable space the player can walk through, not merely observe.

**truth** — Every architectural cavity is a difference. A room is a wall that has been subtracted from.

**emerges** — The bodily understanding that rooms, doors, and windows are all the same operation: solid minus opening.

**critical_parameter** — wall_size against the opening sizes — the ratio of solid to void. Too little solid and the wall stops reading as a wall; too little void and it stops being architecture.

*Reflection.* a placed colour cannot supply its own neighbour, and the gallery cannot hang the neighbour without also hanging a distortion: mis-place the contrast stack and the swatch reads as another colour, and nothing in the hall reports it.

#### Primitive Assembler

`primitive_assembler`

> assemblage is a verb. A stack is not an object you find — it is an

**essence** — an assembly line for the DNA primitives. A conveyor carries raw primitives — sphere, cube, cone — in from the left; one at a time each lifts off the belt and snaps onto a growing vertical stack on the assembly pedestal. The "+" of assemblage made watchable: you see the sum being computed, not just the finished totem.

**desire** — to turn a static composition back into the algorithm that made it. primitive_stack.build() runs a y_cursor loop in a single frame; this externalises that loop into space and time so the player FEELS the addition: ● then ● + ■ then ● + ■ + ▲.

**truth** — assemblage is a verb. A stack is not an object you find — it is an addition you run. The belt is the plus sign.

**emerges** — the bridge artifact. input = primitives (theme 1); belt motion = transform/array (themes 2-3); assembled colour stack = colour assemblage (theme 4); "a rule that runs the belt itself" = the sculpture gallery. One station narrates the spine.

**critical_parameter** — mode — constant | gradient | relational. The same belt, three readings of colour: a property, a function of index, a relation to the neighbour. The colour theme's whole bloom, on one machine.

#### Primitive Combo Puzzle

`primitive_combo_puzzle`

> you cannot button your way to a tower. Composition you have to balance

**essence** — a grab-and-stack combo puzzle for the DNA primitives. A target "sign combo" is posed — ● + ▲ + ▮ — and a tray of FREE, grabbable physics primitives is dealt. Pick them up with your hands and stack them on the plinth in the right order. Get the sequence (and hold it steady) and the combo is solved — then a fresh one is dealt.

**desire** — to make assemblage a thing your hands do under gravity. The assembler runs the "+" for you; the puzzle makes you earn it, piece by physical piece, against wobble and order.

**truth** — you cannot button your way to a tower. Composition you have to balance.

**emerges** — order under gravity. A stack is a sequence you must also keep standing — composition is precarious, and that precarity is the lesson.

**critical_parameter** — combo_length — how many primitives in the target stack. Longer = a taller, more precarious tower to read and hold.

#### brick_wall_factory

`brick_wall_factory`

> a brick wall is a color sequence made solid — the mortar is the silence between notes

**essence** — grid(width, height) of BoxMesh bricks colored sequentially from a named palette — running bond pattern

**desire** — to see a color palette become architecture, each brick a swatch in a wall you can walk alongside

**truth** — a brick wall is a color sequence made solid — the mortar is the silence between notes

**emerges** — the running bond offset (half-brick shift on odd rows) creates diagonal color rivers through the palette sequence

**critical_parameter** — palette — selects from 20 named palettes (starry_night to industrial_brutalism), redefining the wall's emotional register

*Reflection.* a brick wall is a colour sequence made solid, but the factory does not choose the sequence — it reads one of twenty named palettes, so the room that sells colour as self-authorship hands you a vocabulary already written.

### grid pallet  <span>·</span>  `Color_Grid_Pallet`

#### gridcolorizer

`gridcolorizer`

> the name moved, the behaviour didn't

**essence** — thin alias so the historical GridColorizer name still resolves

**desire** — zero behaviour change after the substrate split

**truth** — the name moved, the behaviour didn't

**emerges** — the original behaviour, byte-for-byte, via the new shared base

**critical_parameter** — none — all behaviour delegated to GridColorMutator

#### spectrum_forest

`spectrum_forest`

> the spectrum is continuous but we discretize it into lines

**essence** — hue(x) = remap(x, bounds) — 5000 vertical lines colored by x-position across the visible spectrum

**desire** — to walk through a rain of color, each step shifting the hue around you from violet to red

**truth** — the spectrum is continuous but we discretize it into lines — the gaps between lines are the same lie as RGB

**emerges** — the density of lines creates an accidental fog effect where distant colors blend perceptually, demonstrating optical mixing

**critical_parameter** — line_count — at low counts the spectrum is sparse and abstract, at high counts it becomes an immersive curtain

*Reflection.* the forest is a spectrum only while its gaps stay too fine to see: continuity is rented from line_count, and lowering the density does not blur the colour, it shows there was never anything between the lines.

### rainbow  <span>·</span>  `Color_Rainbow`

#### Mario Cube

`mario_cube`

> joy is not the presence of color — it is the moment darkness becomes color

**essence** — event -> destroy(DarkSphere) + reveal(Rainbow) — one-shot state transition from darkness to color

**desire** — to punch a block and break the darkness open, rainbow erupting where void used to be

**truth** — joy is not the presence of color — it is the moment darkness becomes color

**emerges** — the elastic tween on the rainbow and the back-ease shrink on the sphere create a visual call-and-response that feels choreographed

**critical_parameter** — _activated — boolean gate that ensures the transformation happens exactly once, making it feel like a revelation

#### rainbow

`rainbow`

> a rainbow has no location — it exists only in the geometry between sun, water, and eye

**essence** — ROYGBIV arc(inner_radius + i*thickness) — concentric half-circle bands from ArrayMesh, primary + fainter secondary

**desire** — to stand beneath a double rainbow and feel the scale of spectral order arching overhead

**truth** — a rainbow has no location — it exists only in the geometry between sun, water, and eye

**emerges** — the reversed color order of the secondary rainbow is physically accurate and usually surprises people

**critical_parameter** — SECONDARY_FADE — controls how visible the second rainbow is, teaching that double rainbows exist with reversed color order

*Reflection.* a rainbow has no location, but a map must place it — the arc is a mesh at a fixed radius, so the corridor can show a rainbow only by making it what a rainbow is not: a thing standing somewhere.

#### grab_rainbow_stick

`grab_rainbow_stick`

> grab_rainbow_stick

**essence** — a 1.6 m rod with a grip at one end and a lit bead at the other — the simplest possible extension of the hand, and the first thing in the curriculum that makes the arm longer than the arm.

**desire** — to be carried into a place the hand cannot reach and to report back — the trail behind the bead is the report, written in the air.

**truth** — an extension of the body is never neutral. The moment a stick is fitted at its ends it stops being a length and becomes a claim about what the arm was missing.

**emerges** — a bare shaft reads "go and touch that"; a graduated one reads "go and measure that"; a fulcrum reads "go and move that"; a needle reads "look there"; a bound grip reads "hold this and be seen holding it".

**critical_parameter** — office — what the rod is FOR. A rod is not one tool; it is the ancestor of five, and which one it is shows entirely in what is fitted at its ends.

**desire** — to walk a colonnade where each pillar is a different color, feeling palette relationships through spatial rhythm

#### Color Sets Overview

`color_sets_overview`

> naming a color does not capture it — but it makes the gap between name and experience …

**essence** — palette_atlas — N palettes laid out as grabbable sticker grids, each sticker labeled with hex + nearest web color name

**desire** — to pick up a color, read its name, and carry it across the room — making color portable and personal

**truth** — naming a color does not capture it — but it makes the gap between name and experience visible

#### SpectrumVisualizer

`SpectrumVisualizer`

> the color wheel is a lie the brain tells to make the spectrum feel complete

**essence** — wavelength_to_color(lambda) animated between linear layout (physics) and circular layout (perception) — the spectrum closes into a wheel

**desire** — to watch the visible spectrum bend from a line into a circle, magenta dots filling the gap that physics leaves empty

**truth** — the color wheel is a lie the brain tells to make the spectrum feel complete — magenta is the seam

**emerges** — magenta dots appearing in the gap reveal that magenta is not a spectral color — it is the brain's invention to close the loop

**critical_parameter** — cycle_duration — controls how long you dwell in physics-mode vs perception-mode, pacing the conceptual shift

### paint  <span>·</span>  `Color_Paint`

#### Pattern Mill

`pattern_mill`

> the fold order is not a setting on this machine. Cycle the group and the mirror rotors are rebuilt to match it, so the symmetry can be counted in the hardware before it appears in the cloth

**essence** — motif_grid(NxN) -> wallpaper_group -> live carpet. An editable manufacturing machine: paint a handful of cells on the head, watch spinning mirror rotors multiply the fragment, and the mill feeds out endless symmetric fabric. The symmetry IS the visible mechanism.

**desire** — to fuse the editor and the machine — pattern_maker_station edits, pattern_loom manufactures; the mill does both, and makes the group's fold order legible as rotating mirror blades you can watch change when you cycle.

**truth** — a pattern is a program plus a fragment. Hand someone the fragment and the press — and the rotors that show them the rule turning — and they discover that structure comes from constraint, not complexity. You mill architecture from almost nothing.

**emerges** — the federated move — reuse WallpaperGroups.get_symmetric_color + PatternSim.PALETTES, wrap them in an interactive apparatus with a stroke.

**critical_parameter** — wallpaper_group — the symmetry operator. A few painted cells, read through one of 17 groups, become a carpet. Cycling the group re-counts the rotor blades (2/3/4/6 fold), so the program is written into the machine's body.

*Reflection.* where two splats land on each other the room must decide whether paint is pigment or light — an action-painting studio running on a display inherits a mixing rule from neither, and ships one nobody in the room can check.

#### albers_wall_gallery

`albers_wall_gallery`

> a square inside a square is not decoration — it is an experiment in how context changes the perception of every color it …

**essence** — 98 procedural Albers "Homage to the Square" paintings — nested squares with golden-ratio hue walks and inset_ratio^i scaling

**desire** — to stand before a gallery wall and see how color relationships change when squares nest inside squares — Albers' life work, proceduralized

**truth** — a square inside a square is not decoration — it is an experiment in how context changes the perception of every color it touches

**emerges** — the vertical_bias shifts inner squares downward, creating a gravity effect that makes the paintings feel grounded without explicit composition rules

**critical_parameter** — inset_ratio — controls how quickly inner squares shrink; at 0.78 the nesting feels balanced, at extremes it collapses or barely nests
