#### Color Space Navigator

`color_space_navigator`

> a color is a position — color_space_navigator proves this by placing every achievable hue at its exact coordinate in a cube you can …

**essence** — a navigable 3D color space — RGB cube with wireframe edges, 8 corner spheres for primary/secondary colors, and ~500 sample points filling the interior; optional HSL cylinder shown alongside

**desire** — to make color theory spatial — the player stands inside the RGB cube and sees every achievable color as a point in space; the abstract three-channel model becomes a room you can walk through

**truth** — a color is a position — color_space_navigator proves this by placing every achievable hue at its exact coordinate in a cube you can inhabit

**emerges** — the companion — when show_hsl_cylinder is true, both models appear side by side, making the transformation between color spaces a spatial comparison rather than a formula

**critical_parameter** — cube_size (0.7m default) — scales the entire color space; sample_count controls point density; show_hsl_cylinder toggles the HSL companion model beside the RGB cube

*Reflection.* colour becomes a place you can walk only if it has walls: the cube holds every hue three channels can reach and has no coordinate for the rest, so what the gamut clipped cannot even be pointed at from inside.

#### color_constellation_office

`color_constellation_office`

> color mixing is not a formula — it is what happens when light from different sources arrives at the same place at the same …

**essence** — RGB/CMY glass walls at angles — additive color mixing through transparency and emitted light overlap

**desire** — to walk between colored glass partitions and see your shadow split into complementary colors on the floor

**truth** — color mixing is not a formula — it is what happens when light from different sources arrives at the same place at the same time

**emerges** — walking through overlapping RGB walls creates white-ish zones where all three primaries combine — additive mixing proven by architecture

**critical_parameter** — mixture — WHERE the colours are claimed to meet. The shipped office stages the additive/subtractive pair as two groups of panes and then renders both the same way; the axis makes the room commit to one answer.

#### Subpixel Display

`subpixel_display`

> Every pixel on every screen is three tiny lights pretending to be one color

**essence** — additive color mixing at the hardware level — red + green + blue = everything

**desire** — drag three sliders, watch three bars glow, see the mixed color emerge above

**truth** — Every pixel on every screen is three tiny lights pretending to be one color.

**emerges** — understanding that white is all channels full, black is all off, and every color is a recipe

**critical_parameter** — emission_energy on each bar — intensity maps directly to channel value 0-1

#### Gradient Interpolator

`gradient_interpolator`

> Interpolation is not neutral — the space you choose shapes every color you pass through

**essence** — the path between two colors depends on which space you walk through

**desire** — pick two colors, watch 16 cubes paint a gradient, toggle RGB/HSV and see the path change

**truth** — Interpolation is not neutral — the space you choose shapes every color you pass through.

**emerges** — RGB gradients can look muddy; HSV gradients pass through the rainbow — same endpoints, different journeys

**critical_parameter** — interpolation mode — RGB walks a straight line in a cube, HSV walks an arc on a cylinder

## 4 · change — *F_order*

> Foundation - the calculus substrate. Derivative as instantaneous rate, integral as accumulation, vector field as flow. The vocabulary forces / wavefunctions / randomness / noise will all use.

### intro  <span>·</span>  `Change_Intro`

#### Derivative Pair

`derivative_pair`

> the derivative is not a number at a point — it is a function in its own right

**essence** — two stacked panels, f(x) above and f'(x) below, scrubbed by one shared sweep line — the derivative shown as a curve, not a number

**desire** — to make the player feel that the rate of change has its own shape — steep f means high f', a peak in f means f' crosses zero

**truth** — the derivative is not a number at a point — it is a function in its own right, and the shape of the rate is the shape of the change

**emerges** — the player reads two curves as one statement — where the top rises steeply the bottom rides high, where the top peaks the bottom crosses zero

**critical_parameter** — curve — which f is on the table, and therefore what its rate has to say

#### Slope Tangent Demo

`slope_tangent_demo`

> the slope at a point is the rate of change there

**essence** — a point traces y=sin(x) while a tangent line at that point shows the local slope dy/dx=cos(x) — steep where the curve climbs, flat at the peaks

**desire** — to let the player watch instantaneous rate appear alongside the thing changing, so "slope" stops being a static line and becomes a moving fact

**truth** — the slope at a point is the rate of change there — and a curve carries a different rate at every point it passes through

**emerges** — the tangent reads as the curve's mood — racing up, easing flat at the crest, plunging down — the rate made visible

**critical_parameter** — amplitude — controls how steep the curve gets, and how dramatically the tangent tilts

*Reflection.* the slope is quoted, not measured: it reads cos(x) because the curve was chosen as sin — so this room shows a rate only for functions already differentiated on paper, and never takes the limit it is named for.

### velocity  <span>·</span>  `Change_Velocity`

#### Velocity Arrow

`velocity_arrow`

> velocity is the derivative of position — motion is the visible rate at which where-you-are becomes …

**essence** — a puck circles a closed track with an arrow riding on it, pointing along motion, its length proportional to speed — velocity as the derivative of position

**desire** — to let the player meet velocity before forces names it, so "force changes velocity" lands on something already understood

**truth** — velocity is the derivative of position — motion is the visible rate at which where-you-are becomes where-you-are-next

**emerges** — on tight curves the arrow swings fast, on straights it holds steady — the player sees that changing direction is itself a change in velocity

**critical_parameter** — speed — how fast the puck circulates, and therefore how long and how quickly-rotating the arrow becomes

*Reflection.* the arrow points along a chord to a point 0.05 radians ahead: the puck must be read where it has not arrived, and the chord cuts the corner hardest at the bend the artifact exists to show.

### slope surface  <span>·</span>  `Change_Slope_Surface`

#### Partial Derivative Terrain

`partial_derivative_terrain`

> a derivative in more than one dimension is not one number but a choice of direction

**essence** — a bumpy height-field z=f(x,y) with two arrows riding the surface — a red ∂f/∂x and a green ∂f/∂y — reading both partial slopes at the sampled point

**desire** — to make two-variable differentiation visible as two independent directional slopes on one surface, not a formula on a page

**truth** — a derivative in more than one dimension is not one number but a choice of direction — you differentiate with respect to whichever axis you think to ask about.

**emerges** — two slopes at one point — the seed of the gradient vector that forces' 3D calculus and ML's gradient descent later stack into a single arrow

**critical_parameter** — height_scale — how pronounced the terrain's bumps are; too flat and the partials vanish, too steep and the arrows overshoot the surface they measure

*Reflection.* the two arrows measure the hill only along axes someone laid over it — rotate the grid and both numbers change while the ground does not: a partial derivative is a property of the question, not the surface.

### accumulation area  <span>·</span>  `Accumulation_Area`

#### Integral Area

`integral_area`

> the integral is accumulation — where the derivative asks how fast

**essence** — vertical strips fill the area under a curve left to right — the definite integral painted as it accumulates

**desire** — to make accumulation felt as a growing quantity, the mirror image of the derivative's instantaneous rate

**truth** — the integral is accumulation — where the derivative asks how fast, the integral asks how much has gathered under the curve

**emerges** — the filled region reads as a single number growing — the player sees "how much has happened so far" rather than "how fast"

**critical_parameter** — curve × tally — which f is being gathered, and what form the gathered amount is put in front of you as

*Reflection.* the smooth fill is eighty strips: the room promises a boundary swept continuously and ships a comb fine enough to pass for one, so its continuity is an integer anyone can raise or lower.

### accumulation riemann  <span>·</span>  `Accumulation_Riemann`

#### Riemann Pump

`riemann_pump`

> area under a curve has no single recipe — left

**essence** — three side-by-side strip panels summing the same curve's area by left, right, and midpoint rectangles, pumping the strip count up and down to show them converge

**desire** — to show that accumulation is a choice of strategy at coarse resolution but one shared area in the limit

**truth** — area under a curve has no single recipe — left, right, and midpoint all reach it, and their disagreement at coarse scale is the integral announcing itself.

**emerges** — the difference between a sum and an integral, made spatial — three biases converging to one number

**critical_parameter** — n_strips — the partition fineness; at low counts the three panels' biases diverge visibly, at high counts they collapse onto the true integral

*Reflection.* the exact area is a limit no pump can reach — this hall runs four strips to thirty-two and asserts where infinitely many would land, so every integral downstream is a promise nobody here audits.

#### riemann_pi

`riemann_pi`

> riemann_pi

Visual parameters

--- STAGE-2 DNA (promoted 2026-08-05) ---------------------------------------

WHAT WAS WELDED SHUT. This is not a Riemann SUM — nothing here partitions an
interval. It is the prime counting function pi(x) with the zeta horizon standing
at infinity behind it, and its whole subject is the prime number theorem: the
primes fall unpredictably, but their COUNT follows a law. The shipped exhibit
drew only half of that. It drew the staircase and the scatter and the mystical
wave, and it never drew the law, so the claim the object exists to make was in
the title and not in the picture.

`law` is which smooth estimate stands against the staircase, and the answer is
not decoration: the two classical estimates disagree with pi(x) in OPPOSITE
DIRECTIONS and by an order of magnitude in size.

  none         the staircase alone — the legacy exhibit, byte for byte. The
               count is a fact with nothing to be measured against.
  x_over_log   x / ln x, the crude PNT estimate. At the shipped max_x = 2000 it
               reads 263.1 against the true 303, so it runs visibly BELOW the
               staircase the whole way and the gap widens: a shaded wedge of
               about 6.3% of the plot's area, 2.0 world units deep at the right.
  logint       Li(x) = the integral of dt/ln t, the good estimate. 313.8 against
               303 — it hugs the staircase from ABOVE, a thin band of 2.1%. The
               same law, told properly.
  both         both bands at once, so which estimate is better stops being a
               sentence and becomes a difference of areas.

The band and its curve are ADDED, never substituted, and `none` builds nothing
whatever — which matters more here than usual, because of the finding below.

### flow field  <span>·</span>  `Flow_Field`

#### Vector Field Grid

`vector_field_grid`

> a field is not one arrow but a rule that hands you an arrow wherever you stand

**essence** — a regular grid of arrows over a plane, each pointing along a stream-function field v=(∂Ψ/∂y,−∂Ψ/∂x), the whole field slowly scrolling

**desire** — to present a field — a vector assigned to every point of a domain — as a single seeable object

**truth** — a field is not one arrow but a rule that hands you an arrow wherever you stand — the world laid out before anything moves through it.

**emerges** — circulation lines around saddle points — the flow-along intuition the forces sequence will inhabit

**critical_parameter** — spacing — the grid density; too sparse and the circulation reads as noise, too dense and the arrows overlap into a smear

*Reflection.* a field owes a vector to every point and can afford ninety-six arrows: between them the rule is taken on trust, from a stream function chosen by someone the room never names.

### flow particle pulse  <span>·</span>  `Flow_Particle_Pulse`

#### Particle Flow Swarm

`particle_flow_swarm`

> a field is invisible until a body moves through it — the particle is how the field speaks

**essence** — many small particles carried along the same stream-function field vector_field_grid draws, re-spawning across the plane so the flow reads as continuous streamlines

**desire** — to show that a vector field acts on whatever rides it — the field's effect, not just its shape

**truth** — a field is invisible until a body moves through it — the particle is how the field speaks.

**emerges** — streamlines without stored trails — the field made kinetic, foreshadowing settling-as-riding-a-gradient in the integration phase

**critical_parameter** — particle_count — the swarm density; too few and streamlines never form, too many and the MultiMesh floods the plane into a blur

*Reflection.* the trails are not memory: each is the field integrated backwards from where the particle stands now, so under the scrolling field the room hands every body a past it never had.

### reconciliation  <span>·</span>  `Change_Reconciliation`

#### FTC Bridge

`ftc_bridge`

> differentiation and integration are the same bridge walked in opposite directions

**essence** — an arched deck whose shape is the antiderivative f, standing on pillars at a and b whose height difference is f(b) − f(a), with the rate f' and its signed area drawn underneath

**desire** — to stage the Fundamental Theorem of Calculus as a spatial crossing — the two directions of change meeting over one arch

**truth** — differentiation and integration are the same bridge walked in opposite directions — to accumulate a rate is to recover the thing that was changing.

**emerges** — derivative and integral shown as inverse operations — the capstone that unifies the whole change sequence

**critical_parameter** — curve — which antiderivative the deck traces, and therefore whether the theorem this bridge is labelled with says anything at all

*Reflection.* the beam reads f(b) − f(a) over a deck that is a symmetric parabola, so the ends sit level and the theorem the chapter closes on says 0 = 0 in twenty-six rooms.

## 5 · forces — *oscillation*

> Newton's laws - vectors become physics

### vectors act1 whatavectoris  <span>·</span>  `Vectors_Act1_WhatAVectorIs`

#### Vector Normalization

`vector_normalize_demo`

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### VectorBasics

`VectorBasics`

> A vector is not a number and not a point. It is a displacement

**essence** — v = |v| * v-hat. A vector is magnitude times direction. Decompose into (x,y,z) components.

**desire** — To grab an arrow and stretch it — watching magnitude, unit vector, and components all update as one system.

**truth** — A vector is not a number and not a point. It is a displacement — a difference between two positions, carrying both how far and which way.

**emerges** — The magnitude arc dotting from the x-axis toward the vector tip. The spring scale responding to vector length like a physical instrument.

**critical_parameter** — The dragged endpoint of vector_a. Its position simultaneously defines magnitude, direction, and all three components.

*Reflection.* keeping only which-way requires dividing by how-far, so the one arrow this hall cannot show is the one with no length: direction is separable everywhere except at zero, where the operation quietly fails.

#### Length Lantern

`length_lantern`

> A magnitude is not a number you are told. It is a diagonal you can see

**essence** — |v| = sqrt(x^2 + y^2 + z^2). Length is the diagonal of the box your three components frame. Pull the glowing lantern through the air and the machine measures how far it reaches from the origin — drawing its own right-triangle scaffolding in light.

**desire** — To make length something you hold. The lantern brightens as it reaches farther; a vertical ruler fills; the number in your hand climbs.

**truth** — A magnitude is not a number you are told. It is a diagonal you can see the construction of.

**emerges** — the realisation that 3D length is two Pythagoras theorems stacked — one on the floor, one rising to meet it.

**critical_parameter** — the lantern-tip position v. Its three component legs (x,y,z) square and sum under one root. sqrt nests inside sqrt: the floor diagonal d_xz = sqrt(x^2+z^2) feeds the final rise to |v|.

### vfm 02 operations  <span>·</span>  `VFM_02_Operations`

#### Vector Workbench

`VectorWorkbench`

> a vector is not a number — it is a direction with commitment

**essence** — a universal vector workbench — two draggable 3D vectors with live computed outputs for addition, subtraction, dot product, cross product, and projection

**desire** — to make linear algebra touchable — the player grabs Vector A and Vector B in VR, moves them through space, and watches the dot product change as the angle between them changes; the formula is not read, it is felt

**truth** — a vector is not a number — it is a direction with commitment; VectorWorkbench proves this by making the commitment graspable and showing what two directions do to each other

**emerges** — the parallelogram — when cross product mode is active, a filled parallelogram appears between A and B showing the magnitude of A×B as an area; the geometric interpretation of the cross product becomes visible space

**critical_parameter** — current_mode (Mode enum) — switches the display to highlight one operation at a time; ALL shows every result vector simultaneously, focused modes hide the others so the learner sees one relationship clearly

*Reflection.* the bench computes all five operations every frame and cannot show them at once - the focused modes exist to hide four results so one becomes legible; clarity here is subtraction.

#### Angle Between Vectors

`exercise_5_9_angle_between`

> Two thin arrows rise from a common centre in the XY plane with a pink figure drawn between them and a live degree readout above

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Work-Energy Demo

`work_energy_demo`

> Dot product as physical work — force dotted with displacement gives energy transfer

**essence** — W = F . d = |F||d|cos(theta). Work is the dot product of force and displacement. Perpendicular force does zero work.

**desire** — To let the learner push a ball and see a work meter tick up — then push perpendicular and watch it stay at zero. The dot product made visceral.

**truth** — Pushing sideways does nothing. Work is the component of force that agrees with motion. Everything else is wasted.

**emerges** — Total work accumulating as an integral over the path. Negative work when force opposes motion. The work meter as a running dot-product integrator.

**critical_parameter** — The angle between force and displacement. At 0 degrees all force does work; at 90 degrees none does; at 180 degrees work is negative (energy extracted).

#### Torque Demo

`torque_demo`

> Cross product as torque — force applied at a lever arm produces rotation perpendicular to both

**essence** — tau = r x F. The cross product. Torque is perpendicular to both the lever arm and the force. The right-hand rule determines spin direction.

**desire** — To let the learner drag both the force and its application point — and watch the purple torque vector pop out perpendicular, spinning the ball.

**truth** — Torque is not a force. It is the force's opinion about rotation, measured by how far off-center and how perpendicular it acts.

**emerges** — Zero torque when force passes through center (r parallel to F → cross product vanishes). Maximum torque when force is perpendicular to the lever arm.

**critical_parameter** — force_offset (the lever arm r). Force through the center produces zero torque. The further off-center, the more spin per unit force.

#### The Adder's Drafting Board

`adder_board`

> Addition is not about arrows. It is the fact that two displacements taken

**essence** — a + b = (a.x+b.x, a.y+b.y). Place b's tail at a's tip; the resultant reaches from origin straight to b's new tip. The tip-to-tail chain and the parallelogram are the SAME truth, drawn two ways.

**desire** — To compose. To drag two heading-arrows end to end and see the diagonal you'd actually travel light up in gold the instant it lands on a grid line.

**truth** — Addition is not about arrows. It is the fact that two displacements taken in sequence equal one displacement — and that the detour is never shorter.

**emerges** — The triangle inequality, discoverable while dragging: |a+b| <= |a|+|b|, with a brass needle that pegs to equality only when a and b go parallel.

**critical_parameter** — The two grabbable tip-pucks (a's tip, b's tip). Everything else — the resultant, every number, the parallelogram ghosts — snaps to the math the same frame. Inputs you move; results you never touch.

#### Stretch Bench

`stretch_bench`

> To multiply a vector by a number is to choose a length and a direction-sign

**essence** — (k*v) = (k*vx, k*vy, k*vz). One scalar dial drives one vector. Scalar multiplication only ever touches LENGTH and SIGN — never the line the vector lies on. |k*v| = |k| * |v|, always.

**desire** — To turn one brass crank and watch the twin arrow stretch past v's tick, shrink back through the origin, collapse to a glowing dot at k=0, and flip 180 degrees through its own tail when k goes negative.

**truth** — To multiply a vector by a number is to choose a length and a direction-sign on a line you have already drawn. The line is given; the dial chooses where on it.

**emerges** — the realisation that the amber twin always lies on v's line — scaling is not rotation. Negative k is reversal, |k|>1 is stretch, |k|<1 is shrink, k=0 is collapse. The etched ghost-lattice (-2v..3v) is the field the dial sweeps.

**critical_parameter** — k in [-3, +3]. The three landmarks — k=+1 (identity, the twins coincide), k=0 (collapse to the origin dot), k=-1 (perfect mirror) — are the whole lesson, felt as detents under the hand.

#### Agreement Gauge

`agreement_gauge`

> The dot product does not measure length. It measures how much two

**essence** — a·b = |a||b|cos(theta) = ax*bx + ay*by + az*bz — one scalar that says how much two directions agree. Two arrows, a self-substituting three-line equation, and a red->grey->green rail along which a physical puck slides to cos(theta). The number is computed, never grabbed.

**desire** — to make alignment legible without reading a digit — open the angle and the puck slides green, snap to 90 deg and it parks at the grey notch (dot=0), pull past 90 and the rail goes red (dot negative).

**truth** — The dot product does not measure length. It measures how much two directions agree. At 90 degrees, they are strangers.

**emerges** — the geometric form |a||b|cos(theta) and the algebraic component sum land on the SAME number, stacked and visibly equal — sign and magnitude in one read.

**critical_parameter** — angle theta between a and b. 0 deg -> dot peaks at |a||b|; 90 deg -> dot snaps to 0 ("strangers"); 180 deg -> dot is minimal (opposition).

#### Vector Addition (a + b, two-pad console)

`vector_add`

> Vector addition made playable - the entry operation of the embodied vectors-forces arc

**essence** — two 2D pads, one per vector. Drag a pad and that vector's tip follows your hand; the two arrows draw head-to-tail and the resultant swings live. The control IS the tip.

**truth** — a + b is not two numbers added twice — it is where you arrive if you walk a, then b; a − b is where b's tip must reach to land on a's.

#### Dot-Aligner (aim · foe = mercy)

`dot_aligner`

> The dot product made playable, and the embodied vectors-forces arc's operations toy

**essence** — aim a turret at a drifting foe; the dot of (aim · direction-to-foe) is the charge; full alignment locks a beam and converts the foe FOE -> FRIEND.

**truth** — alignment is a number you can feel — point at the thing and the angle closes; the dot product is how much two directions agree, and agreement here is mercy, not a kill.

#### Vector Translation

`vector_translation_demo`

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Coordinate System Switcher

`coordinate_system_switcher`

> Shows the same 3D point in Cartesian (x,y,z), Cylindrical (r,θ,z)

**essence** — One point, three descriptions: (x,y,z), (r,theta,z), (rho,theta,phi). The point does not change; only the language does.

**desire** — To show that coordinate systems are choices, not facts — the same white dot described three different ways simultaneously.

**truth** — A coordinate system is a way of speaking, not a way of being. The point exists before any frame names it.

**emerges** — The yellow cylindrical radial line and the magenta spherical rho line meeting at the same point — different decompositions of the same position.

**critical_parameter** — point_cartesian — moving it rewrites all three readouts and redraws all projection lines and arcs.

#### Basis Vectors Rig

`basis_vectors_rig`

**essence** — P = x*i + y*j + z*k. Any point is a weighted sum of basis directions.

**desire** — To decompose space into understandable parts — grab a point and see its coordinates unfold along three axes.

**truth** — Coordinates do not belong to the point. They belong to the relationship between the point and the frame.

**emerges** — The realization that coordinates are not absolute — they depend on which basis you choose. Rotating the basis changes the numbers but not the point.

**critical_parameter** — target_point — the Vector3 being decomposed. Moving it rewrites the linear combination in real time.

#### Vector Subtraction

`vector_subtraction_demo`

**essence** — A - B = A + (-B). Subtraction is addition of the opposite. The difference vector points from B's tip to A's tip.

**desire** — To show that subtraction is not removal — it is the directed gap between two positions.

**truth** — The difference between two positions is a direction. Subtraction creates the arrow that connects them.

**emerges** — The ghost arrow -B appearing at A's tip makes the tip-to-tail construction visible. Subtracting identical vectors yields zero — visually collapsing to a point.

**critical_parameter** — The relationship between vector_a and vector_b. When equal, the result is zero — the gap closes.

#### Dot Product Projector

`dot_product_projector`

**essence** — A . B = |A||B|cos(theta). The dot product measures alignment. Positive = same direction, zero = perpendicular, negative = opposed.

**desire** — To let the learner rotate two arrows and watch a number change sign — making orthogonality a felt threshold, not a definition.

**truth** — The dot product is the question: how much do these two directions agree? The answer is a scalar — dimension collapses by design.

**emerges** — The perpendicular drop line from A's tip to the projection. The angle arc sweeping through the sign change at 90 degrees.

**critical_parameter** — The angle between vector_a and vector_b. At 90 degrees the dot product crosses zero — the phase transition of alignment.

#### Vector Projection

`vector_projection_demo`

**essence** — reflect(A) = A - 2(A.n)n. Projection strips a component; reflection mirrors through a plane. Both are dot-product machines.

**desire** — To let the learner tilt a plane and watch a vector bounce — making the law of reflection a spatial experience, not a memorized rule.

**truth** — A mirror does not reverse left and right. It reverses the component perpendicular to its surface. Reflection is subtraction, twice.

**emerges** — Angle of incidence equaling angle of reflection — visible in the symmetry of A and its reflection about the translucent plane. Glancing angles produce near-tangent reflections.

**critical_parameter** — The normal vector direction. It defines the plane. Tilting it changes what "perpendicular" means and rewrites both projection and reflection.

#### VectorTorque

`VectorTorque`

> Torque is leverage made mathematical — the cross product measures how much of your force is wasted pushing toward the pivot

**essence** — τ = r × F; |τ| = |r||F|sin(θ); moment arm r_perp = r - (r·F̂)F̂; torque is the rotational force around a pivot, magnitude proportional to the moment arm length

**desire** — to feel why a long wrench works better — radius_vector and force_vector combine via cross product to show how distance from pivot multiplies the turning power

**truth** — Torque is leverage made mathematical — the cross product measures how much of your force is wasted pushing toward the pivot, and gives you only the turning remainder.

**emerges** — the moment arm r_perp — visible as the green vector, it is the part of r that is perpendicular to F, and its length is what actually determines the torque magnitude

**critical_parameter** — the angle between r and F — at 90° torque is maximum (force fully perpendicular to lever arm); at 0° it is zero (pushing along the arm does nothing)

#### VectorSubtraction

`VectorSubtraction`

> Subtraction is directional memory — a-b is the vector that

**essence** — a - b = a + (-b) — flip b to its negative, then add; difference_vector points from b's tip to a's tip

**desire** — to show that subtraction is relabeled addition — -b is b reversed, and the balance_beam gadget makes the equivalence felt rather than memorized

**truth** — Subtraction is directional memory — a-b is the vector that, if you followed b, would tell you where a is.

**emerges** — the direction between two tips — a-b always points from b's tip to a's tip, making it the vector "from B to A" and giving subtraction spatial meaning

**critical_parameter** — negative_b — the negated vector rendered in parallel shows why a-b ≠ b-a; switching which is negated flips the direction of the result

#### Vector Addition Walk (Room Scale)

`vector_addition_walk`

> You don't observe vector addition. You stand inside it

**essence** — A + B = C at body scale. You ARE inside the vector space.

**desire** — Walk along the X-axis to feel the X-component. Addition becomes spatial, not symbolic.

**truth** — You don't observe vector addition. You stand inside it.

**emerges** — The parallelogram from ghost arrows. Component projections along axes.

**critical_parameter** — Grid size vs vector length — vectors must fit inside the walkable volume.

*Reflection.* standing inside addition requires the sum to fit indoors - the walk-in twin caps every arrow at the room's reach, so an operation true at any magnitude is walkable only at small ones.

#### example_2_3_gravity_scaled_by_mass_vr

`example_2_3_gravity_scaled_by_mass_vr`

> Gravity treats mass as both source and target

**essence** — Gravity computed as a force scaled by each body's mass, revealing that all masses fall at the same acceleration

**desire** — To enact Galileo's experiment — drop different masses, watch them stay together, see why F=mg cancels in the equation of motion

**truth** — Gravity treats mass as both source and target — and so cancels itself in motion, leaving acceleration alone to fall. ===========================================================================

**emerges** — The equivalence principle as visible event — heavy and light reach the floor together, the algebra confirmed by the eye

**critical_parameter** — gravity_strength — the constant that proves itself constant by producing equal acceleration regardless of mass

**truth** — a projection is the part of one vector that lives along another — the shadow it casts; what's left over is what makes it different.

### vfm 05 launch  <span>·</span>  `VFM_05_Launch`

#### Catapult

`catapult`

> Velocity is what you give. Gravity is what is given. The arc is their sum

**essence** — p(t) = p0 + v0*t + 0.5*g*t^2. A launch is an initial condition. Set the angle (direction) and the force (magnitude) and the parabola is already written — you only watch it play out.

**desire** — To fling. To pull two levers and watch a stone trace the arc you composed.

**truth** — Velocity is what you give. Gravity is what is given. The arc is their sum.

**emerges** — The realisation that the velocity arrow + the constant gravity arrow ARE the parabola. Aim becomes prediction.

**critical_parameter** — The release velocity = launch_dir(angle) * force. Direction and magnitude together. After FIRE, gravity alone bends the rest.

*Reflection.* the whole range is one assignment - linear_velocity = aim * power - and everything after it is consequence; an arc cannot be corrected, only re-aimed and fired again.

#### Turret-Boll Vector Diagram

`turret_boll_infoboard`

XR ToolsViewport 2D in 3D

This script manages a 2D scene rendered as a texture on a 3D quad.

Pointer and keyboard input are mapped into the 2D scene.

#### Firework Burst (projectile fountain)

`firework_burst`

> The firework as pure projectile motion - a mortar throws sparks up in a cone and gravity arcs every one into a parabola

**essence** — a fountain of projectiles launched in a cone; each is its own launch vector bent by the same g, and together they bloom into an umbrella that widens and rises with the burst. Low burst is a tight column, high burst a broad canopy of falling embers.

**truth** — a firework is many straight throws sharing one gravity — the bloom is what a fan of vectors looks like when the world pulls them all the same way.

#### Firework Launcher

`firework_launcher`

> A firework is a population explosion. One event becomes many independent trajectories

**essence** — launch → ascend → explode(N particles) → each: v += g, v *= drag, life -= dt, fade. One event becomes sixty trajectories.

**desire** — To launch a rocket and watch it bloom — sixty glowing particles inheriting random velocities from a single explosion point, then falling under gravity and drag.

**truth** — A firework is a population explosion. One event becomes many independent trajectories, each obeying the same law, each taking a different path.

**emerges** — Ring patterns from constrained emission (horizontal only). Sphere patterns from uniform random direction. Particle trails tracing parabolas. The moment of bloom as maximum expansion.

**critical_parameter** — shell — the figure the burst throws (sphere | ring | spokes | willow), the pyrotechnic shell type made reachable from a map; burst_force (1.0-8.0) sets how wide it opens

#### Drag Lane (F = -b·v you feel)

`drag_lane`

> Friction / drag made felt - a forces toy for the embodied vectors-forces arc

**essence** — a runner enters a resistant medium at full tilt and slows; frozen stroboscopically, its snapshots bunch together and its velocity arrows shrink as the drag eats the motion. Run through air, water, honey — each pulls back harder.

**truth** — drag is a force that only ever opposes — it never starts you moving, it only spends what you already had; the faster you go, the harder it takes it back.

#### Slingshot Launcher

`slingshot_launcher`

> A brief force applied once writes a whole trajectory

**essence** — A charging platform detects a body standing on it, builds power, then catapults it along a parabolic arc — impulse you feel in your own trajectory

**desire** — To collapse the distance between reading "impulse = force × time" and being the projectile that the impulse launches

**truth** — A brief force applied once writes a whole trajectory — change nothing after launch, and the arc was already inevitable at the moment of release. ===========================================================================

**emerges** — The full parabola of projectile motion appears from a single burst — after release no force steers you but gravity, yet the path was decided in the launch instant

**critical_parameter** — launch_force and launch_angle_deg — together they set range and height; 45° maximises distance, steep angles trade reach for altitude

#### Force Pad

`force_pad`

> a launch is just a velocity you're given; gravity does the rest

**essence** — a 1x1 m launch pad — step on it and it throws you UP and FORWARD on a ballistic arc. A force vector you trigger with your feet.

**desire** — to make impulse bodily — the instant you touch the plate, gravity hands you a velocity (up + forward) and the room rushes past.

**truth** — a launch is just a velocity you're given; gravity does the rest.

**emerges** — locomotion as a vector sum — the chevrons + the glowing arrow show the exact direction you'll fly before you commit your feet.

**critical_parameter** — up_force + forward_force — the two components of the launch velocity v0 = forward*f̂ + up*ŷ; together they set the arc and the range.

#### Return Launcher (the way home)

`return_launcher`

> The catapult made sci-fi and made personal: instead of flinging a stone toward +Z it flings YOU on a ballistic arc back to where you started

**essence** — stand on the glowing launch ring and the energy arm snaps over; a velocity is solved on the spot — p(t) = p0 + v0·t + ½g·t² — so that t seconds later you land exactly on your spawn. It can't throw the wrong way, because it always aims at the beginning.

**truth** — a launch is an initial condition; pick the landing and the velocity is forced. The machine doesn't aim in a compass direction — it aims at home.

#### Mortar Vector Siege

`mortar_vector_siege`

> You aim the launch; gravity aims the landing. Hit the foot of the curve

**essence** — v0 = aim_dir * force, then p(t) = p0 + v0*t + 0.5*g*t^2. You set the direction by TILTING your hand and the magnitude by HOLDING the trigger. The shell's whole arc — and the patch of ground it will scorch — is already written the instant you let go. Aim is prediction.

**desire** — To lob. To tip the tube, feel the charge swell, release, and watch a shell fall on a drone you could not have hit by pointing straight at it.

**truth** — You aim the launch; gravity aims the landing. Hit the foot of the curve.

**emerges** — The felt difference between a direct-fire vector (turret: point AT it) and an indirect-fire vector (mortar: aim a parabola so its FOOT lands on it). Gravity is not the enemy of the shot — it is the shot.

**critical_parameter** — aim_dir (hand tilt) * _force (charge time). Direction and magnitude together pick where the parabola crosses the floor — the AoE ring.

#### Human Catapult

`human_catapult`

> the surest way to understand a launch vector is to BE the projectile

**essence** — a catapult that launches YOU. Walk into the basket, set the angle and force, pull FIRE, and the arm flings the VR player up and away on a real ballistic arc — F=ma made personal.

**desire** — to put the player inside the vector. The teaching catapult throws a ball at the trajectory; this one throws the player ALONG it, so the parabola is felt in the body, not watched from outside.

**truth** — the surest way to understand a launch vector is to BE the projectile.

**emerges** — locomotion as lesson — gravity owns you the instant you leave the cup.

**critical_parameter** — launch_force + launch_angle — the v0 of p(t)=p0+v0*t+½g*t². Together they set where you land; the live arc previews it before you commit.

#### Circle Train (centripetal force, a = v²/r)

`circle_train`

> Centripetal force made playable - a sci-fi intermezzo for the embodied vectors-forces arc

**essence** — velocity is always tangent to the ring; the force is always inward; and the trick that makes circular motion feel alive is that the inward force grows with the SQUARE of speed — double the speed, quadruple the pull. The train leans into it.

**truth** — going in a circle is constant acceleration toward a centre you never reach — straight-line desire bent by a force that only ever points sideways.

#### Normal Force & Reflection Demo

`normal_force_demo`

> Vector projection and reflection — decompose velocity into normal and tangent components

**essence** — F_normal = (F.n)*n, F_parallel = F - F_normal. Gravity decomposes into the force that holds you up and the force that makes you slide.

**desire** — To show that one force becomes two when it meets a surface — the part the surface resists and the part that causes motion.

**truth** — The surface does not push you up. It pushes you perpendicular to itself. Gravity does the rest.

**emerges** — The ball accelerating faster on steeper surfaces. The normal force vector shrinking as the parallel one grows — a conservation visible in arrow lengths.

**critical_parameter** — surface_angle — steeper angle means more parallel component, more sliding. At 0 degrees all force is normal; at 90 degrees all force is parallel.

#### 3D Bouncing Ball

`exercise_1_3_solution_3_d_bouncing_ball_vr`

> A bouncing ball is a clock that runs down. Each bounce measures how much the universe …

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Motion 101 Velocity and Random Acceleration VR

`example_1_9_motion_101_velocity_and_random_acceleration_vr`

> The walk is not random. The DRAW is random; the walk is the only thing the law could have done with …

**essence** — A ball whose acceleration is redrawn from a uniform box every frame, integrated into velocity and position

**desire** — To show that a random walk is not disorder — it is one law applied honestly, over and over, to a number nobody chose

**truth** — The walk is not random. The DRAW is random; the walk is the only thing the law could have done with it. ===========================================================================

**emerges** — A path that looks intentional without any intention in it — the tank fills with a scribble no line of code drew

**critical_parameter** — the +/-0.02 acceleration box and the 0.15 speed cap — together they decide jitter, drift, or a saturated pinball

#### Accelerate and Decelerate

`exercise_1_5_solution_accelerate_and_decelerate_vr`

> smooth arrival is not a decision — it is a feedback loop

**essence** — steer = (desired - velocity).clamp(max_force). Seek + arrival. Distance to target scales desired speed.

**desire** — to watch a ball chase a moving target, accelerating when far and decelerating when close — smooth arrival from pure math

**truth** — smooth arrival is not a decision — it is a feedback loop. The closer you get, the gentler the approach.

**emerges** — smooth deceleration zone near the target where speed ramps down proportionally to distance

**critical_parameter** — slowing_radius — the only line that separates arrival from seek. Inside it, desired speed is scaled by d/r.

#### VectorThrowing

`VectorThrowing`

> A throw is an initial condition. After release

*This work has no words of its own. Its header is empty.*

#### Bouncing Ball

`bouncing_ball`

> The quintessential physics demo: a ball bouncing under gravity with configurable coefficient of restitution

**essence** — Bounded chamber of bouncing balls — physics engine handles gravity, collision, restitution; we only set initial conditions

**desire** — To let the player watch deterministic physics produce apparent randomness through accumulating collision micro-differences

**truth** — A bounce is not random — it is the moment when constraint becomes visible, when geometry refuses to let the ball pass.

**emerges** — Newton's laws as the engine, visible cube as the stage — the chamber's regularity reveals each ball's path as deviation

**critical_parameter** — ball_count and PhysicsMaterial.bounce — together they decide whether the box becomes still, periodic, or chaotic

#### Pointing in Direction of Motion

`example_3_3_pointing_in_the_direction_of_motion_vr`

> A thing that points where it moves has surrendered its identity to its velocity

**essence** — look_at(position + velocity). A cone that always points where it is going. Orientation derived from velocity, not set independently.

**desire** — To show that direction of motion IS orientation — the cone's nose traces its velocity vector, making heading a consequence of movement, not a choice.

**truth** — A thing that points where it moves has surrendered its identity to its velocity. Heading is not chosen; it is inherited from motion.

**emerges** — The cone's orientation lagging behind direction changes, showing inertia. Smooth curves from continuous thrust. The mover tracing invisible Lissajous figures.

**critical_parameter** — thrust_magnitude (0.01-0.15) — controls how quickly the mover changes direction. Low thrust = gentle curves. High thrust = sharp turns.

*Reflection.* the cone's heading is look_at(position + velocity), its orientation on loan from its motion - so stop it and there is nothing to point at; a body that faces only where it goes has no face at rest.

#### Arbitrary Angular Motion

`example_3_2_forces_with_arbitrary_angular_motion_vr`

> Rotation does not need a force. It needs a torque

**essence** — a += F/m, v += a, p += v, omega += random, theta += omega. Linear attraction plus independent angular motion. Two dynamics, one body.

**desire** — To show that rotation and translation are independent — boxes orbit an attractor while spinning at their own rate, two freedoms coexisting.

**truth** — Rotation does not need a force. It needs a torque — or random impulses. Translation and rotation are independent stories told by the same body.

**emerges** — Movers wrapping around boundaries and re-entering from the opposite side (toroidal space). Spin rates diverging as random angular impulses accumulate.

**critical_parameter** — angular_damping (0.9-0.999) — controls how quickly spin decays. Low damping = chaotic spinning. High damping = gradual alignment. The knob between chaos and order.

#### VectorForces

`VectorForces`

> A force is a direction with a claim. When two forces disagree

**essence** — F_net = gravity + thrust + drag; drag = -DRAG_COEFFICIENT * velocity; a = F_net / mass; velocity integrates acceleration each frame

**desire** — to see Newton's second law in real time — gravity pulls, thrust fights back, drag resists motion, and the ball trajectory shows what the sum decides

**truth** — A force is a direction with a claim. When two forces disagree, the net force is the argument they settle at.

**emerges** — the visible taxonomy of forces — five simultaneous arrows (gravity, thrust, drag, velocity, acceleration) let you watch how each contributes before they combine

**critical_parameter** — DRAG_COEFFICIENT (0.8) — too high and the ball stops immediately; zero and it goes parabolic; the visible drag arrow shows the invisible resistance

#### ForceMagnitudeDemo

`ForceMagnitudeDemo`

> Drag the force arrow longer and the ball accelerates harder

**essence** — F = ma. Drag the force vector longer → ball accelerates harder. Magnitude is felt, not read.

**desire** — To make the learner drag a red arrow and feel the ball respond proportionally — bigger arrow, faster ball.

**truth** — You never feel position or velocity. You feel acceleration. Force is the only thing that reaches your body.

**emerges** — The intuition that force and acceleration are the same shape. The ball's trajectory traces the force's history.

**critical_parameter** — The length of the applied_force_vector. It is the magnitude that drives everything.

#### Combined Forces Demo

`combined_forces_demo`

> Vector addition as force combination — multiple forces acting on a body

**essence** — F_net = F1 + F2. Superposition. Forces add as vectors. The ball feels only the sum.

**desire** — To let the learner drag two force arrows and watch the ball respond to their sum — not to either alone. Superposition as felt experience.

**truth** — Nature does not apply forces one at a time. Every force acts simultaneously. The body knows only the sum.

**emerges** — Cancellation when forces oppose. Diagonal trajectories from perpendicular forces. The net force arrow always predicting the ball's next move.

**critical_parameter** — The angle between F1 and F2. Parallel forces compound; perpendicular forces create diagonal motion; opposing forces cancel.

#### Trajectory Artifact

`trajectory_artifact`

> Placeable force-trajectory wrapper. Integrates the configured force system (Lorenz

**truth** — "A FORCE IS NOT A PUSH — IT IS THE WHOLE FAMILY OF PATHS IT WOULD CARVE" applications: trajectory integration, force fields, phase portraits, batched MultiMesh rendering, seeing a dynamical system as the set of all its possible histories.

#### example_2_5_fluid_resistance_vr

`example_2_5_fluid_resistance_vr`

> Drag is the medium's memory of your velocity. The faster you move

**essence** — F_drag = -C * |v|^2 * v-hat. Drag opposes motion and scales with speed squared. Heavier objects fall faster through the same fluid.

**desire** — To drop five balls of different masses into a blue fluid and watch the heavy ones punch through while the light ones float — mass as privilege in a resistive medium.

**truth** — Drag is the medium's memory of your velocity. The faster you move, the harder the world pushes back.

**emerges** — Terminal velocity — each mover reaches a speed where gravity and drag balance. Heavier movers reach higher terminal velocity. The cyan drag arrows grow with speed.

**critical_parameter** — drag_coefficient — higher C means thicker fluid. It determines terminal velocity and how quickly the fluid swallows kinetic energy.

#### Friction Ramp

`friction_ramp`

> Friction is not a force that always opposes motion

**essence** — An inclined plane with tunable angle and friction — block slides when tan(θ) > μ, sticks when tan(θ) ≤ μ

**desire** — To make the static-friction inequality visible as a threshold event — the slope where motion suddenly begins

**truth** — Friction is not a force that always opposes motion — it is the contract surfaces sign before they decide whether to let go.

**emerges** — Friction reveals itself as a binary boundary — hold or slide — emerging from continuous parameters at a sharp threshold

**critical_parameter** — friction_mu and ramp_angle_deg — their ratio decides whether the block holds or releases

#### Collision Crasher

`collision_crasher`

> momentum is always conserved -- the impulse arrows prove it whether you believe the math or …

**essence** — impulse = m * delta_v -- central body with 5 tethered blocks demonstrating momentum conservation

**desire** — blocks crash and bounce on tethers, impulse arrows tracing each collision's physics

**truth** — momentum is always conserved -- the impulse arrows prove it whether you believe the math or not.

**emerges** — Newton's third law made ambulatory -- every action-reaction pair visible as arrow vectors

**critical_parameter** — tether length / block mass -- constrain the collision space; impulse arrows show transfer

#### Newton's Cradle

`newton_cradle`

> In a closed line of equal masses, momentum cannot be created or destroyed

**essence** — Five suspended balls trade momentum through elastic collisions — lift one, release it, and exactly one leaves the far end

**desire** — To make conservation of momentum audible and visible, so a conserved quantity stops being an equation and becomes a click-clack you can count

**truth** — In a closed line of equal masses, momentum cannot be created or destroyed — it can only be handed down the chain. ===========================================================================

**emerges** — A one-in-one-out rule no ball was told to obey falls out of equal masses and elastic contact alone

**critical_parameter** — damping — at near-1.0 the cradle rings for minutes; lower it and the swing decays, revealing that "conservation" is always an idealisation energy leaks from

#### Half-Life Turret - Vector Compendium

`hl_turret_vectors`

**essence** — D = T - P, cos(theta) = F.D/|F||D|, V = D-hat * speed, P' = P + V*dt. Nine vector operations in one machine.

**desire** — To make every vector operation serve a purpose — subtraction finds the target, dot product checks the FOV, cross product aims the barrel. Nothing is abstract here.

**truth** — A turret does not think. It subtracts, normalizes, dots, crosses, and integrates. Intelligence is arithmetic applied with purpose.

**emerges** — Leading the target — bullets aim at predicted position using ball velocity. Hit accuracy emerging from the interplay of fire rate, bullet speed, and ball chaos.

**critical_parameter** — field_of_view_degrees — the cone of awareness. Narrower FOV = more precise but more blind spots. The dot product threshold that separates "acquired" from "searching."

*Reflection.* the turret's arithmetic is complete and decides nothing - where "acquired" begins is a dot-product threshold someone set, so the neutral part is the maths and the chosen part is the number it is compared to.

#### vector_drone

`vector_drone`

> The drone is a mirror of the turret. Same math

**essence** — desired = (target - position).normalized() * speed. Chase, prepare, shoot, cooldown. A state machine driven by vector subtraction.

**desire** — To hunt. To orbit at ideal_distance, charge projectiles, fire in bursts, and kamikaze when dying — an adversary that teaches vectors by trying to kill you.

**truth** — The drone is a mirror of the turret. Same math, opposite role. Subtraction works for both hunter and hunted.

**emerges** — The drone's strafing pattern from sinusoidal offsets. Leading shots via look_at. Kamikaze desperation creating final-moment tension.

**critical_parameter** — ideal_distance (4.0 units) — the range the drone wants to maintain. Too close = backs up. Too far = approaches. In range = switches to attack states.

### vectors act3 fieldsandmotion  <span>·</span>  `Vectors_Act3_FieldsAndMotion`

#### VectorMotion

`VectorMotion`

> Position, velocity, and acceleration are one quantity seen at three depths of change

**essence** — A moving ball carrying three live arrows — position, velocity, and acceleration — plus a fading trail, so the calculus of motion is shown as nested vectors

**desire** — To make the chain "acceleration changes velocity changes position" visible at once, instead of as three separate equations a student must mentally integrate

**truth** — Position, velocity, and acceleration are one quantity seen at three depths of change — each is the rate at which the one below it is rewritten. ===========================================================================

**emerges** — The relationship between the three vectors becomes legible — velocity always tangent to the trail, acceleration always bending it — without any formula on screen

**critical_parameter** — acceleration — it is the only thing directly driven; velocity is its running sum and position is velocity's sum, so the top of the chain governs the whole arc

*Reflection.* a vector that stops holding still needs both extent and history, and the ladder has one slot per room - so the map is filed on motion while half of it belongs to rung five.

#### Launch Arc (projectile motion)

`launch_arc`

> Projectile motion made playable - the launch / F=ma toy for the embodied vectors-forces arc (the pad that throws you)

**essence** — a launch pad fires at angle θ with speed v; gravity pulls the path into a parabola. The launch velocity decomposes into a horizontal vx and a vertical vy — vx carries you, vy fights gravity, the trade between them sets the range.

**truth** — you only ever launch a straight vector; the curve is the world's answer.

#### Weather Vector Field — Storm Chamber

`weather_vector_field`

> A walkable storm chamber (~5x5 m) built on vector addition

**essence** — R = A + B; F_rain = gravity + wind. Vector addition drives the field, the instruments, and every GPU particle system at once. Superposition made meteorological — a whole storm chamber that agrees on one vector.

**desire** — To let the learner walk inside a storm, drag two wind arrows, and watch real rain slant, debris streak downwind, clouds and mist drift, lightning strike downwind — all at the resultant angle, while the classic instruments read the same vector.

**truth** — Wind is a vector field. Rain is a particle advected through two fields at once. Every instrument AND every particle in a storm is a different read of the same vector.

**emerges** — Rain streaking diagonally as the decomposition display shows wind + gravity = trajectory. Debris flung downwind as the headline vector in flight. Lightning forking out of a storm sky, biased downwind. A blizzard of slow white snow when the mode flips.

**critical_parameter** — gravity_strength — it competes with wind. High gravity = rain falls nearly vertical; low gravity = rain flies nearly horizontal. The ratio is the slant angle, and the rain emitter's gravity is literally down + wind.

#### Force Vortex

`force_vortex`

> a force field is a velocity assigned to every point in space

**essence** — a rotating force field — a whirlpool that drags you toward its core, spins you around it, then flings you back out. Force as a place.

**desire** — to make a vector field bodily — not an arrow you read but a current you are caught in: inward pull + tangential spin = a spiral you ride.

**truth** — a force field is a velocity assigned to every point in space.

**emerges** — centripetal vs centrifugal felt in the gut — the field owns you, then releases you faster than you came in.

**critical_parameter** — pull_strength + spin_strength set the spiral; eject_force + eject_up set the throw. v_in = -r̂*pull + (ŷ×r)*spin; v_out = r̂*eject + ŷ*up.

#### Bubble Blaster (output velocity vector)

`bubble_blaster`

> A soap-bubble gun that fires a spreading cone of bubbles and wears its launch vector on the muzzle - an embodied force-display prop

**essence** — pull the trigger and bubbles pour out a spreading cone; the muzzle wears an arrow for the launch speed and a faint cone for the spread. Crank the output and the arrow stretches, the cone widens, the bubbles fly faster and further.

**truth** — every emitter is a little vector field — each particle leaves with a velocity, and the "force" of a spray is just that launch vector, made of thousands of tiny departures.

*Reflection.* every prop here works and none of them means anything yet - a working artifact depends on an arrangement it cannot make for itself, and this is the room that admits it has none.

#### VectorFieldFlow

`VectorFieldFlow`

> A vector field is a set of instructions written in space

**essence** — F(p) = swirl(p) - radial(p). A vector field assigns a direction to every point. The particle reads the field and follows.

**desire** — To make an invisible flow visible — 81 arrows breathing in unison, a particle tracing the field's will.

**truth** — A vector field is a set of instructions written in space. The particle has no memory and no plan — it only reads the local instruction.

**emerges** — Spiral trajectories from the combination of rotational swirl and inward radial pull. The particle's path is never a straight line.

**critical_parameter** — The swirl-to-radial ratio in _field_value(). It determines whether the field spirals inward, orbits, or ejects.

*Reflection.* the throw is the datum and the arrow is only its transcript, so this map depends on a body it cannot ship: a learner who does not throw gets the formalisation with nothing underneath it.

#### Force Field Visualizer

`force_field_visualizer`

> Motion is reading, not deciding. The field tells the particle where to go

**essence** — F(x) = vector at every point. The field precedes the particle. Space is not empty.

**desire** — To show the invisible. To make the learner see that empty space carries instructions.

**truth** — Motion is reading, not deciding. The field tells the particle where to go.

**emerges** — the two axes are orthogonal on purpose — `law` changes what is true of the space and `evidence` changes how much of the working is admitted, so the 4x4 grid asks whether a law is easier to read when the instrument shows more of itself.

**critical_parameter** — Field type (gravity/coulomb/dipole/vortex). Each is a different spatial grammar.

*Reflection.* the resultant is a description of the result, not a cause of it - the body meets only the sum, and superposition gives it no way to decline a contributor: every pull counts, and the count is addition.

#### Magnetic Field Simulation

`magnetic_simulation`

> Magnetic field simulation with dipoles, field line visualization

Magnetic Simulation — RigidBody3D magnets with force-based attraction/repulsion
Uses apply_central_force() for magnetic interaction, GPUParticles3D for field lines

*Reflection.* a field claims an arrow at every point, but field_at() rounds your position to a cell and hands back that cell's vector - between the samples there is no field, only the nearest one already stored.

#### vector_field

`vector_field`

> A field is a decision made everywhere at once

**essence** — A 3D lattice of arrows that each point the way the field flows at that location, with particles advected through it — a function from space to direction, made walkable

**desire** — To show that a vector field is not arrows decorating space but a rule assigning every point a push, by letting you stand inside one and watch particles obey it

**truth** — A field is a decision made everywhere at once — give every point a direction and motion through space becomes obedience to a map you can see. ===========================================================================

**emerges** — Coherent global flow — drains, swirls, separatrices — arises purely from a local direction rule evaluated independently at each grid point

**critical_parameter** — field_type — switching between Vortex, Saddle, Source, Sink, Tornado, Wave, and Circular completely rewrites where the flow carries you

#### Force Fields

`force_fields`

> A force field is a region with different rules

**essence** — Area3D.gravity_space_override = COMBINE. Three zones, three rules: directional push, radial pull, viscous drag. Same balls, different fates.

**desire** — To throw balls into colored zones and watch physics change — one zone pushes sideways, one sucks inward, one floats with resistance. Territory as force law.

**truth** — A force field is a region with different rules. Cross the boundary and the laws change. Physics is local.

**emerges** — Balls orbiting the point attractor. Balls floating in the wind/drag zone as buoyancy balances gravity. Balls ricocheting in the directional field.

**critical_parameter** — gravity and linear_damp per zone. The point attractor's gravity (20.0) vs the wind zone's damp (4.0) — pull vs resistance, two ways to trap a ball.

#### Particle Systems

`particle_systems`

> Particle emitters spawning thousands of particles with configurable lifetimes

**essence** — emit(position, velocity, lifetime). Each particle: born, forced, aged, dead. The system is the population, not the individual.

**desire** — To show four archetypal particle behaviors side by side — fountain (ballistic), fire (buoyant), sparks (explosive), snow (gentle) — all from the same GPU machinery.

**truth** — A particle system is a statistical object. No single particle matters. The population's distribution is the phenomenon.

**emerges** — Fire looking like fire despite being spheres — because upward gravity + high damping + color ramp = buoyant fading embers. The color gradient does most of the work.

**critical_parameter** — gravity direction and damping per system. Fountain: gravity down, low damp. Fire: gravity UP (buoyancy), high damp. These two parameters create completely different phenomena.

*Reflection.* population behaviour depends on a mechanism that does not travel - the loop holds four aggregations and no rule for getting from one to the next; the incompatibility is what teaches.

#### Vector Fields

`vector_fields`

> 3D vector field visualization with arrow glyphs showing direction and magnitude at grid points

**essence** — F(p) = sum(sources). Each point samples the superposed field of all Area3D gravity sources. Arrows point where particles would go.

**desire** — To place an attractor and a repulsor and watch the field tear itself between them — arrows bending, particles caught in the tug of war.

**truth** — A field is not a picture of arrows. It is a promise: if you put a particle here, this is what will happen.

**emerges** — Saddle points between attractor and repulsor where the field cancels. Particle streams forming visible flow lanes. Color gradient mapping field magnitude.

**critical_parameter** — The ratio of attractor gravity (+8) to repulsor gravity (-6). It determines whether particles orbit, escape, or get trapped.

### vectors act4a kineticforces  <span>·</span>  `Vectors_Act4a_KineticForces`

#### Drag Corridor (walk-in air/water/honey)

`drag_corridor`

> The body-scale, walk-in twin of drag_lane: a corridor of three media (air

**essence** — drag is proportional to velocity and against it; the denser the medium the bigger b, so the same launch goes far in air, less in water, barely a lunge in honey. The probes freeze the decay stroboscopically — their velocity arrows shrink as they bunch up.

**truth** — drag is the world charging you rent on speed — pay in honey, glide free in air.

*Reflection.* nothing here resists on its own: drag is b times your velocity and b belongs to the medium, so the corridor slows you by deciding what the air is made of, not by anything about you.

#### Orbit Walk (walk-in two-body)

`orbit_walk`

> The body-scale, walk-in twin of orbit_pair: stand at the barycenter while a heavy star and a lighter planet wheel around you on opposite …

**essence** — the two bodies pull on each other equally and oppositely (Newton's third law) and orbit a common centre of mass; make one heavier and the barycenter slides toward it, the orbits resizing to keep the balance. F = G m₁m₂/r².

**truth** — an orbit is two things falling toward each other and missing, around a centre that belongs to neither — and here you are standing on it.

#### Centrifuge Ring (walk-in centripetal)

`centrifuge_ring`

> The body-scale, walk-in twin of circle_train: a pod laps a big neon ring while the velocity (tangent) and centripetal force (inward

**essence** — velocity is always tangent to the ring; the force is always toward the centre you never reach. Crank the speed and the velocity arrow stretches while the inward arrow explodes — a = v²/r is why fast turns throw you.

**truth** — going in a circle is constant acceleration toward a centre you never reach.

#### Force Mower (W = F d cos θ)

`force_mower`

> The lawn-mower work diagram turned into a thing you push - an embodied force-display prop for the vectors-forces arc

**essence** — push a mower by its angled handle; the push F splits into the part that does work (F cos θ, along the ground) and the part wasted into the dirt (F sin θ, downward). Steeper handle → more of your push goes nowhere. The vectors live on the object.

**truth** — only the part of a force that lies along the motion does any work — push down on the world all you like, the lawn doesn't care; it only feels what goes forward.

#### Wedge Slide (inclined-plane free body)

`wedge_slide`

> The inclined-plane free-body problem made playable - a primitive on a wedge

**essence** — dial the incline; a primitive sits on the slope while its forces draw themselves in 3D, and the monitor renders the flat free-body section — the textbook triangle with gravity, normal, friction and the two components, live. Tilt past tan θ = μ and it slides.

**truth** — a slope doesn't change gravity — it changes how much of gravity points along your path; the whole inclined-plane problem is one vector, resolved into the two that matter.

### vectors act4b oscillation  <span>·</span>  `Vectors_Act4b_Oscillation`

#### Bounce Well (restitution)

`bounce_well`

> Restitution made playable - a console toy for the embodied vectors-forces arc (the clean rebuild of the bouncing-ball sim)

**essence** — a ball dropped into a well bounces, and each bounce returns to e times the last height; frozen stroboscopically, the arcs shrink geometrically and bunch toward the rest. e = 1 is a perpetual bounce, e = 0 a dead thud.

**truth** — a bounce is a negotiation with the floor — how much of your fall you are allowed to keep; restitution is the floor's mercy, between nothing and everything.

*Reflection.* a pendulum keeps time as T = 2pi*sqrt(L/g), so the hall's clocks are calibrated by the planet beneath them - the steadiest instruments in the sequence cannot name a second without borrowing a constant the room does not hold.

#### Momentum Cradle (p = mv conserved)

`momentum_cradle`

> Momentum conservation made playable - Newton's cradle

**essence** — lift the end ball and let it fall; the blow travels through the still middle balls untouched and kicks the far ball out to the same height. The momentum you put in comes straight out the other side — nothing lost in the balls between.

**truth** — momentum is a debt the world always repays in full — what passes in must pass out, and the quiet middle is just the courier.

#### Weather Vane (wind ∝ v²)

`weather_vane`

> Wind made playable - F_drag = ½ρv²C_dA. A ToyConsole

**essence** — a weather vane swings to point downwind while a flag streams off the pole; the wind's force grows with the SQUARE of its speed, so a doubling of wind quadruples the push — the flag goes from a lazy ripple to a horizontal snap.

**truth** — wind is just air with somewhere to be; the force is in the speed, squared, and a flag is a little instrument that integrates it for you.

#### Lever (τ = F·d)

`lever_balance`

> The law of the lever made playable - τ = F·d, balanced when F₁d₁ = F₂d₂

**essence** — a beam on a fulcrum with a fixed weight on one arm; slide the load along the other arm and you change its TORQUE — force times distance — not its weight. Far out, a small load outweighs a big one near in; the beam tips toward the bigger torque and sits level only when the two are equal.

**truth** — a lever trades force for distance and never gets something for nothing — what it multiplies in force it pays for in the distance the far end must travel.

#### Pendulum Hall (walk-in)

`pendulum_hall`

> The body-scale, walk-in twin of pendulum_swing

**essence** — a long arm hangs a heavy bob from a tall pivot; it swings on the actual physics (ω = √(g/L)), the restoring force mg sin θ hauling it back through bottom-dead-centre, and a glowing arc on the floor records the path it sweeps. Stand in the hall and the force vectors ride the bob at the scale of your own body.

**truth** — a pendulum keeps time because the pull back grows with how far it's pulled — and at this size you don't read that off an arrow, you feel the bob pass.

#### Spring Tower (walk-in Hooke)

`spring_tower`

> The body-scale, walk-in twin of spring_bob: a heavy mass bobs on a giant coil at its real period T = 2π√(m/k)

**essence** — the spring returns force in exact proportion to how far it's stretched, so the mass oscillates forever about equilibrium — F = −kx, the signature of everything that keeps time. Stiffen it (the slider on the small twin) and it bobs faster, gives less.

**truth** — a spring is honest; it gives back exactly what it's given, and that is why it ticks.

#### Calder Mobile (project primitives)

`calder_object_mobile`

> The balanced Calder mobile (τ = w·d at every arm) but hung with the project's own ordinary objects - point

**essence** — a binary tree of steel arms. Build a node's two children first, weigh them, then place the pivot so w_left·d_left = w_right·d_right; the heavier child rides the shorter arm. Recurse, and the whole thing balances from a single point at the ceiling.

**truth** — a mobile is the lever law made into art — Calder's genius was that the weights and the lengths are doing real arithmetic while they drift.

*Reflection.* the mobile balances the curriculum's own shapes by tau = w*d, but a point and an arch have no weight - each is handed a plausible mass, so a real equilibrium is computed over invented quantities.

#### Calder Mobile (balanced, real weights)

`calder_mobile`

> A large hanging Calder mobile that is actually balanced

**essence** — a binary tree of steel arms. Build a node's two children first, weigh them, then place the pivot so w_left·d_left = w_right·d_right; the heavier child rides the shorter arm. Recurse, and the whole thing balances from a single point at the ceiling.

**truth** — a mobile is the lever law made into art — Calder's genius was that the weights and the lengths are doing real arithmetic while they drift.

#### Mass-Spring-Damper

`mass_spring_damper`

> Every vibrating system is a mass-spring-damper in disguise

**essence** — F = -kx - cv. Spring restores, damper resists. Three regimes: underdamped (oscillates), critically damped (fastest return), overdamped (sluggish return).

**desire** — To pull three masses down in VR and release — watching one ring like a bell, one snap back efficiently, one creep home reluctantly. Damping ratio as personality.

**truth** — Every vibrating system is a mass-spring-damper in disguise. The damping ratio is the system's temperament.

**emerges** — The underdamped mass overshooting and ringing. The critically damped mass returning fastest without overshoot. The overdamped mass returning slowest. Three personalities from one equation.

**critical_parameter** — damping coefficient d (0.5, 12.0, 40.0). It alone determines the character: d < d_critical = ringing, d = d_critical = optimal, d > d_critical = sluggish.

#### Spring System

`spring_system`

> A spring network is a medium. Perturbation at one point travels through connections

**essence** — F = -k(|x|-L)*x-hat. A network of masses connected by Hooke's law springs. Each spring pulls its endpoints toward rest length.

**desire** — To see a web of springs breathe — wind perturbing it, colors shifting with tension, the whole system finding equilibrium and losing it again.

**truth** — A spring network is a medium. Perturbation at one point travels through connections. Structure is communication.

**emerges** — Tension waves propagating through the network. Color shifting from calm blue to hot pink as springs stretch. Fixed anchor points creating standing wave patterns.

**critical_parameter** — spring_stiffness (k=50) and damping (0.95). Higher stiffness = faster oscillation, tighter network. Lower damping = longer ringing. Together they set the network's texture.

`harmonic_motion_demo`

> Interactive simple harmonic motion demonstration

**truth** — "EVERY OSCILLATION IS THE SAME EQUATION WEARING DIFFERENT NUMBERS" applications: simple harmonic motion, waves, springs and pendulums, Fourier's claim that any signal is a sum of these, damping as energy leaving the system.

#### Slingshot (elastic launch, PE = 1/2 k x^2)

`slingshot_pull`

> The slingshot as stored energy - draw the pouch back and the band stores PE = 1/2 k x^2

**essence** — draw the pouch back and the band stores energy as the square of the pull; let go and that energy becomes launch speed, the projectile arcing away. Pull twice as far and you store four times the energy.

**truth** — a stretched band is a promise — the further you pull, the more the world owes the stone, paid back as the square of your patience.

#### ChainSwing

`ChainSwing`

> A chain swing is a conversation between gravity and constraint

**essence** — anchor → pin → link → pin → link → pin → seat. A chain of rigid bodies connected by pin joints, driven by periodic force.

**desire** — To swing — to feel how constraints propagate force through a chain, each link amplifying or dampening the one above.

**truth** — A chain swing is a conversation between gravity and constraint. Each link votes on where the seat should go.

**emerges** — The double-pendulum whip when the chain catches resonance. The trail drawing the seat's arc through space.

**critical_parameter** — link_count and joint damping — more links = longer period, more damping = faster decay.

*Reflection.* a chain that swings needs one link that cannot: the anchor is the only part held rigid, so a hall about compliance hangs entirely from the one thing that refuses to give.

`surreal_kinetic_sculpture`

**desire** — stage rigid mechanism producing soft consequence — the visible cause-and-effect of art-physics

**truth** — A sculpture is a choreography frozen in materials. Naming the parts after artists (Tanguy / Niki) makes the physics legible as a tradition, not just a simulation.

**emerges** — a quietly working machine where rigid black geometry produces colorful organic motion — aesthetic translation of mechanical work

**critical_parameter** — piston_speed_multiplier × mechanical_precision — together they set the tempo and the precision of the motion

#### HingeCrank

`HingeCrank`

> Every engine is a conversation between rotation and translation

**essence** — hinge(motor) → pin → slider. Rotary-to-linear conversion through composed constraints.

**desire** — To turn a wheel and watch a rod pump — the fundamental crank-slider mechanism.

**truth** — Every engine is a conversation between rotation and translation. The crank is the translator.

#### SliderPress

`SliderPress`

> Sliders translate without rotating. The opposite constraint to the hinge

**essence** — SliderJoint3D — 1 translational DOF. The opposite of a hinge. Linear motion, no spin.

**desire** — To slide. To show that constraining rotation while freeing translation builds pistons, drawers, rails.

**truth** — Sliders translate without rotating. The opposite constraint to the hinge. Together they build machines.

**emerges** — Mechanical rhythm from oscillating motor. The feel of constrained linear motion.
