#### Herringbone Pattern Tile

`pattern_tile_herringbone`

> every repeating pattern in the world — from Islamic tiles to woven textiles to crystal structures

**essence** — preview[px,py] = palette[grid[(py%N, px%N)]] transformed by RepeatMode/WallpaperGroup — a NxN color array tiled infinitely by a symmetry rule, with drag-and-drop colored cubes to edit the source tile

**desire** — to experience pattern as repetition made tangible — to place a single colored cube and watch it propagate across a carpet according to a mathematical symmetry rule, understanding that patterns are small decisions amplified by structure

**truth** — every repeating pattern in the world — from Islamic tiles to woven textiles to crystal structures — is an instance of one of the 17 wallpaper groups, and this artifact makes that classification navigable with your hands

**emerges** — the cursor visualization reveals that the same source cell appears multiple times in the preview — the index math (px%N, py%N) becomes physically visible as a spotlight moving across the carpet, connecting to identical tiles

**critical_parameter** — wallpaper_group (when in WALLPAPER mode) — the 17 mathematical wallpaper groups classify every possible way to tile a plane with a repeating motif, and switching between them changes the character of the whole carpet from a single edit

#### Pattern Maker Station

`pattern_maker_station`

> structure does not emerge from complexity — it emerges from constraint applied to the …

**essence** — symmetry_group(domain[x][y]) -> infinite_carpet — paint a small NxN grid, select one of 17 wallpaper groups, watch the GPU tile it into an infinite-seeming carpet via symmetry operators

**desire** — to touch cells on the upright panel with VR hands and see the floor carpet update in real time — to cycle through P1 to P6M and feel how constraint multiplies a fragment into architecture

**truth** — structure does not emerge from complexity — it emerges from constraint applied to the almost-nothing

**emerges** — the GPU shader carpet (wallpaper_tile.gdshader) with grout, noise distort, and aging parameters produces worn Italian mosaic effects from a handful of colored cells

**critical_parameter** — wallpaper_group — the symmetry operator that transforms a 4x4 domain into a universe; 17 groups, exactly 17, a hard constraint the universe imposes on 2D repetition

*Reflection.* closure needs a set of moves you can pick two of, and the station has none: each group is one hard-coded index formula, so the set closes because the table names it, not because anything here composed.

### seventeen  <span>·</span>  `Symmetry_Seventeen`

#### Wall Pattern Gallery

`wall_pattern_gallery`

> symmetry is not decoration — it is the mathematical structure that determines how a small truth propagates through …

**essence** — for each Z-cluster of wall cubes, overlay all 6 faces with wallpaper_tile shader using a unique wallpaper group + neon palette + procedural domain texture

**desire** — to walk through a tunnel where every surface — walls, ceiling, floor — displays a different wallpaper group in neon color, immersing the body in mathematical symmetry

**truth** — symmetry is not decoration — it is the mathematical structure that determines how a small truth propagates through space

**emerges** — the combination of 8 neon palettes, 6 pattern types (blocks/stripes/diagonal/concentric/checker/cross), and 17 wallpaper groups produces a corridor that never visually repeats

**critical_parameter** — cluster_size — cubes per Z-row before advancing to next wallpaper group; controls how much space each symmetry type occupies

*Reflection.* the closed census needs a lattice that can hold it, and this one is square: twelve groups run exact, and the five hexagonal ones — p6m, the Alhambra's, among them — are float rotations rounded back into square cells.

#### VR Tile Editor

`vr_tile_editor`

> the tile editor is a live proof that a 4×4 array

**essence** — VR workspace = PatternTilePuzzle (tilted editing board at desk height) + live-updating floor carpet (MultiMesh tile texture regenerated on every pattern_changed signal) + optional wall preview panel

**desire** — to design a floor while standing on it — to grab a colored cube, place it on the tilted editing board, and see the tile pattern propagate immediately across the floor beneath your feet, closing the loop between authorship and inhabitation

**truth** — the tile editor is a live proof that a 4×4 array, held in hand, can generate an infinite floor — the editing board is the source code and the carpet is the running program

**emerges** — the live-update loop creates an authorship experience that traditional pattern editors lack — you feel yourself as both designer and inhabitant simultaneously, which embodies the array's core claim about index-to-value mapping

**critical_parameter** — carpet_repeats — controls how many times the source tile repeats across the floor; at Vector2i(8,8) the floor becomes a demonstration of infinite repetition; at (2,2) the relationship between source and repeat is obvious
